### 2.6 The closed vocabularies (each `rg -w` on master `src` · `tests` and the ledger `docs`; `cure/L-BD-probe/census-*.txt`; the register entries are the fold §5 rows)

| Vocabulary | Values | Census verdict |
|---|---|---|
| `BODY_KINDS` | `conclave · gang · beast` | FREE as literals; `conclave` is a PROSE homonym (an institution NAME containing it classes ARCANE in six regexes — the kind never enters a name); `gang` an ALIGNED homonym (criminal-name keywords); `beasts` (plural) a DefenseTab threat key (aligned) and an institution THEME tag meaning livestock (another surface) |
| `BODY_BIRTHS` | `spilled · sent_forth · driven_out · den_held · bred · wild_born · deserted · cast_out` | FREE as literals (prose hits only) |
| `BODY_ENDS` | `starved · scattered · dissolved · merged · raised · disbanded` | `starved`, `scattered`, `dissolved`, `disbanded` are ALIGNED homonyms (the same "ceased" sense: `factionLifecycle.js` `dissolved`, `successionGrammar.js` `scattered`, `institutionLifecycle.js` `disbanded`); `merged`, `raised` FREE as literals |
| `BODY_CHOICES.conclave` | `rally · preach · supplicate · hide · defy · move` + `confront` (a GOOD or warlike god's flock SEEKS the gang that terrorizes its people) · `raid` (a HOSTILE flock's taking act, the gang's own choice word in the same sense) | `confront` FREE (0 quoted; 1 prose `src` file, `foundingSeeds.js`; `cure/D-BD-r5-probe/census-r5.out`); `raid` as the gang's row; the rest FREE as literals; `gather` is ES's operative choice (`espionageGauntlet.js`: `choice: 'gather'\|'govern'`) and `petition` the commons' rung (`commonsVoiceKernel.js :: RUNG_WORD = ['', 'petition', 'gathering', 'riot']`); `grow` is folded into `preach` (the growth is the faith system's share law, BD5) |
| `BODY_CHOICES.gang` | `raid · smuggle · bribe · recruit · feud · move · scatter` | `raid` is a landed INCIDENT type (`relationshipRulesAdversarial.js`: `incidentType: "raid"`) and the registry's `raid` KIND (§12) — here a CHOICE id only, in the same sense (the act that the kind hears); the rest FREE as literals |
| `BODY_CHOICES.beast` | `prowl · roam · lie_up · flee` | FREE — not `hunt` (a self-homonym with the stance toward a beast), `range` (eight UI literals, `type="range"`) or `den` (ST's den and a criminal-name regex) |
| `COURT_STANCES.conclave` | `permitted · curbed · endowed · outlawed` | `curbed` for `suppressed`, the FAITH SURFACE's own word twice over (`religionState.js`'s per-deity `suppressed: false`, a latent evicted deity; `patronFall.js :: PATRON_FALL_CAUSES` `'suppressed'`); `endowed` for `patronized` (`magicRegimeModel.js :: REGIME_PATRONIZED`) |
| `COURT_STANCES.gang` | `tolerated · hunted · employed · harboured` | `tolerated` ALIGNED with magic LEGALITY (`magicProfile.js`: "`'forbidden', 'restricted', 'regulated', 'tolerated', 'celebrated'`" — a court lets a practice be); `harboured` for `sheltered`, a DEFENCE band (`townMapModel.js :: DEFENSIVE_BANDS = ['exposed', 'open', 'mixed', 'sheltered', 'fortified']`) — PL's own word for a court sheltering the crown's enemy, the same act |
| body↔body labels | `allied · neutral · rival · hostile` | REUSED: the landed pair-state ids (`neighbourSlice.js :: RELATIONSHIP_TYPES`), their landed meanings |
| toward a beast | `hunt · avoid` | `hunt` ALIGNED with the tradition id `'hunt'` (`traditionCorpus.js`) and the food institution keyword (another surface); `avoid` FREE as a stance |
| `BODY_SUPPLY_WORDS` | `provisioned · strained · starving` | REUSED: the war's supply band (`warResolve.js :: supplyBand` "`return 'provisioned'`" · `'strained'` · `'starving'`) — the same meaning, a force's supply state |
| might | `thin · modest · capable · strong · formidable` | MN-0's `STRENGTH_BAND_KEYS` (the first of MN-0 and BD-0 to land mints them on `armyStrength.js`) |
| `TRAIL_LEARNED` | `by_catch · by_survey · by_guide · by_broker · by_pursuit · by_dm` (+ the derived source tag `by_office`) | FREE (`by_broker` · `by_pursuit` 0 · 0 · 0, `census-r5.out`); `by_survey` · `by_broker` DARK until ES and IN carry their rows, the words kept |

| the incident | `peace_unkept` (with a `bodyKind` field) · `peace_kept` · `shared_faith` (the near flock's bounded trust lean, §10.2 (a); FREE — 0 · 0 · 0, `census-r5.out`) | One cause family `peace_unkept` with the body kind as its field — never three cause words; one cause for a gang's brigandage, a pack's neglect and a far flock's spread; FREE on master (0 · 0); the SAME word and meaning as PL's REQUESTED-family row `peace_unkept` (a member's land exporting trouble; an obligation by meaning — PL §4.8 (1)) — two records of one fact-family, registered as an aligned pair (the pair's incident · the fealty row) |

One word per end, in the volume's existing words (L-BD 49 (a)'s symmetries): a beast's `died_out` IS `starved`, its `named` IS `raised` (BD16); a conclave's `recognized` and `absorbed` are the FAITH ROW's own landed states `established` (`standingFor`) and `suppressed` (the latent evicted deity, `religionState.js`) — PL's `recognized` is a SEAT recognition and `absorbed` POP's placement response, so neither is minted here; `moved_on` IS the birth `driven_out` (§3.2). |

INT's commons voice has no stance words (its `RUNG_WORD` is the grievance ladder `petition · gathering · riot`); `anticipatedReactions.js :: REACTION_BANDS` (`supportive · indifferent · wary · opposed`) is a COURT's forecast of a power — another meaning, not reused |

| the sponsorship, visible-defence, spill and arrival words | `sponsor_body` (the objective BD-9's car adds to ES's covert decider, §10.4) · `sponsorObjectiveFor` (its door, in ES's doctrine stage) · `sponsorObjectiveScore` (BD's leaf) · `bodyVisibleDefenceOf` (the choice's public defence read, §2.5) · `spills` (the slow spill's dwell map, §2.2) · `commitArrival` (BD's writer's arrival commit, §2.7 step 5) · the raid's population outcome `candidateType: 'body_raid'` (§9.2) | FREE: `sponsor_body` · `sponsorObjectiveFor` · `sponsorObjectiveScore` · `bodyVisibleDefenceOf` · `commitArrival` · `body_raid` 0 `src` · 0 `tests` · 0 master `docs` · 0 ledger `docs`; `spills` a PROSE word in master `src` (0 quoted, 0 key forms), registered (`cure/D-BD-p5-probe/census-p5.out` · `census-p5b.out`); `defensiveTerrainRungOf` (BD-4's byte-identical extract exported from `mapProfile.js`) FREE — 0 `src` · 0 `tests` · 0 master `docs`; `captiveId` (ES-2b's custody key for an errand-less captive — ES's token to register, BD cites) FREE — 0 · 0 · 0 (`cure/D-BD-p5b-p5-probe/census-p5b.out`) |

### 2.7 The stage — `src/domain/bodies/bodyStage.js :: advanceBodies`
A LEAF called at the head of `calamityKernel.js :: advanceCalamity` right AFTER THE PATROLS' stage (which follows ST's, which follows MN's; OD-MN-20, ST §6.1, PR §4.1), before calamity's own flag gate; +3 host lines (the call and the merge of `{ worldState, settlementUpdates, newsEntries, receipts }` — the SETTLEMENT records the guild war's condition, a ruin and a founding write ride `advanceCalamity`'s own `settlementUpdates` channel, "`@param {CalUpdate[]} args.settlementUpdates  this tick's post-apply settlement updates`"); zero kernel lines. Bodies move AFTER the riders are placed, so the week's encounter reads this week's watch (truth) while each body planned on what its ties believed (BD12). Each tick, in this fixed order, every step writing only through `bodyLedger.js` (and through each OTHER owner's one writer where it says so):
1. **Ends from facts** — a body below its existence floor ends `starved` (beast, gang) or `scattered` (gang; and a moving CONCLAVE at zero supply — its people drift to the towns, §6.4) or `dissolved` (conclave, by entry); a gang whose END GOAL is reached ends `dispersed` or `dissolved` into its institution or hamlet (§6.8); a beast with no habitat in reach for a season ends `ranged_away` (§6.9); a gang that chose to join an ENLISTING menace ends `merged`, REQUESTING the absorption through MN's one writer `absorbBody` (§9.8 (d)) — refused `menace_not_enlisting` unless the menace stands `raiding` in the course's country, a counted deserter gang's people leaving by the people ledger's `dm_removed` sink with cause `menace_absorbed` (MN §6); an end the DM ordered applies; every end's id joins the week's ONE `writeHabits` `ends` list (BD-10); each receipted with `causedBy`.
2. **Upkeep and the season** — every body pays its upkeep from `bodySupply01` by size, × the ONE seasonal need factor for all three kinds (winter raises a gang's and a flock's need as it raises a pack's hunger, §6.1); a subservient gang pays its guild's share (§6.8); cohesion decays (slow with a base, fast without; a leader's fall lowers it, §6.4); beasts breed in spring if provisioned, starve if not (§6.3); the season's SPONSORSHIP DELIVERY is drawn once per tie (§10.4).
3. **Births and names** — the slow spill's (N, T) chances are drawn first and a fired one writes its `spills` key (§10.1 (ii); `bodySpillEnabled`); then for each country with habitat over security and room under the cap, each of the five sources tested at its dwell (§3); a body crossing its NAMING BAND is named, one falling below it starts its lapse (BD23, receipts only); a gang over its radius or a pack past its range is tested for FISSION (§6.7).
4. **The weekly choice** — each unpinned body draws ONE choice from its situation-scored set on the stream `bodies:<bodyId>:<tick>` (§4).
5. **Movement** — by the one physics on the ONE road ladder, road or way CHOSEN per move by `bodyRouteChoice` (BD11; stance a prior weight, never the rule), within the radius (§5); a move ONTO A SITE is a REQUEST — the stage calls ST's arrival door `siteStage.js :: arriveAt(worldState, siteId, { kind: 'body', bodyKind, id }, tick)`, which only REGISTERS the arrival for the site's ONE contest this week, and the body's place on the site (its `where`, a new `baseSiteId`) is NOT written; ST's closing call `closeSiteWeek` (the leaf after this stage) returns the per-mover verdict `arrived · held`, and BD's ONE writer COMMITS the place on `arrived` ONLY (`bodyLedger.js :: commitArrival`, in the same chain) — on `held` the record stands where it stood (PS's hold helper's FREEZE: the withheld commit, nothing rolled back) and next week's choice re-decides; each crossing of a way adds ONE to its way-mode edge's `useCount` — a candidate's FIRST crossing mints that edge at `wayRung: 'wild'` through the lifecycle's writer (the desire path's birth) — where `routeLifecycleEnabled` is lit (BD20; the route lifecycle's own write, WY's).
6. **Acts and engagements** — each act either resolves as a receipt (a flow's loss, a recruitment) or mints its registry kind for the week (§9, §12); a flock's ALMS requests to the towns in its radius (§6.6), the courts' BOUNTY and HUNT forks (§9.9, §9.11), a guild war's weekly line (§6.8), a pending sponsorship offer (§10.4) and a ransom race's clock (§9.10) each keep their CLOCKED card for the week (BD13); once a SEASON per court whose `sponsorObjectiveScore` is non-null, the stage CALLS ES's ONE covert decider's `sponsor_body` door (BD-9's car — the caller, never the decider) and mints the offer's card the week after a `sponsor` verdict; the guild war's ONE condition is RE-ASSERTED each week it stands, its lease renewed (§6.8 (d)); a NAMED body's QUARTER ACT resolves as an act receipt (§7.6); the hearings registered with SP-9 resolve docket-first at the NEXT advance (BD hosts no docket pass).
7. **Standings, stances, pairs and lessons** — the gang standings, the court stance rows (and their bounties) and the body pairs move by the week's resolved acts (one writer); each body's and each court's LESSONS are credited through HB's one writer (§4.4) where `habitConditioningEnabled` is lit; the commons' regard moves only through RN's door from the NAMED body's deeds (§7.6).
8. **The word** — Herald and rumor entries with the typed slots; the map projection refreshes.

## §3 WHERE BODIES COME FROM — HABITAT, THE FIVE BIRTHS, THE DWELL AND THE CAP (BD-1; the owner's ~18:3x "do they just magically appear, or do they appear from settlements or dens?", ~18:5x "does one jsut pop up from a village or somewhere where appropriate scaling", ~19:1x "Gangs can also form from some immigrant goups or detached from them")

### 3.1 Habitat minus security — the balance
Each week, per settlement country T and per kind, `bodyBalance(T, kind) = bodyHabitat(T, kind) − bodySecurity(T)` (§2.5; every term a landed read, every weight DRAFT, UNMEASURED). A birth is POSSIBLE only where the balance is above the kind's birth floor (DRAFT 0.35) and the country has room under the cap (§3.4); it is DRAWN, never certain (the keyed draw `bodies:birth:<word>:<placeKey>:<tick>`, odds `BIRTH_BASE × net`, DRAFT), so the prospect is unpredictable and the scrutiny line legible ("the quarter breeds: hungry, the watch thin, the guild strong"). The level FEEDS BACK as habitat (a gang recruits where the roads are already lost) and is bounded by the cap, the dwell and the supplies — never a runaway (requirement 2: the counterforce names itself — the watch, the riders, the keep, the trails learned, the bread).

### 3.2 The five births (each from a landed fact; each a receipt; each sized to its source)

| Birth | Kind | The source fact (a landed read, or a slot) | Size | Where it is born | The receipt |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| (a) FROM A SETTLEMENT — `spilled` | gang | the town's criminal power (`criminalStrength01Of`, the stamp) × unrest × famine (the crime pressure) over the gang birth floor, the dwell passed | by the town's tier: a hamlet's spill `thin`, a city quarter's `modest`, a metropolis's `capable` at most (DRAFT) | the nearest cover country to the town (§5.2), or the town's own quarter when no cover lies within a week (it works the town's roads) | "Out of Kell's quarter, a gang has taken to the hill roads." |
| (a) FROM A SETTLEMENT — `sent_forth` / `driven_out` | conclave | `sent_forth`: the MISSION — a creed ESTABLISHED in a neighbour N (share ≥ `STANDING_ESTABLISHED`) toward a town T where it is absent or a `cult`, odds ∝ N's share × the piety gap (T's local piety over the creed's standing there) × alignment affinity (`stanceOf(T's patron, the creed).cooperation − aggression`) × the road between them (`hopWeeks`), at a dwell; a CHURCH-seated N whose chooser picked `missionize` this season (`settlementStrategy.js`) births it directly ("sent out by Kell's temple"); `driven_out`: a court's `outlawed` turn (§7.1) on a creed's `cult` row, when its flock chooses `move` (§4.2) | the creed's flock: `thin`, `modest` from a city (DRAFT) | at its seat of origin, on the road toward its goal | "The Grey Lady's faithful went out from Kell toward Ashford." · "Driven out of Merewick, the Grey Lady's flock is on the road." |
| (b) FROM A DEN — `den_held` | gang (or beast) | an UNTIED holder of a site (ST §2.1's untied body ref — never a `band` slot — WC-12's ambient raiders' located form) IS a body: at BD-0's adoption every ID-LESS AUTHORED GANG ST placed before C17 becomes a gang record holding that site through ST's writer (no holder change; a menace holder stays MN's); a beast's lair BREEDS by season (`bred`, below) | the holder's might as ST read it (`might` was the DM's for an authored band) | at the site | "The band at the Tolle ruin is the Tolle hill gang." |
| (b) `bred` | beast | a provisioned beast at its lair in spring (§6.3) | one band below the parent (DRAFT) | its lair's country | "The wolves of the Tolle hills have whelped; there are more of them this spring." |
| (c) FROM THE WILD — `wild_born` | beast | the wild's habitat: `config.monsterThreat` (`plagued` > `frontier` > `heartland`, the landed `MONSTER_THREAT_TIERS`) × the country's COVER SHARE (§5.2) × the distance from towns (the wild is wide beside thorps and hamlets) × the season (spring births; winter drives existing packs to the roads, never births); the calamity bucket's beast wave stays the STRIKE (MN11: "A strike is not a presence") | `thin`; `modest` in `plagued` country (DRAFT) | the most-covered country of the region | "Wolves have come down into the Tolle hills." |
| (d) FROM DEFEAT — `deserted` | gang | DARK UNTIL WC-11/12 LAND (the loser's deployment record and the lifted siege's receipt do not carry the defeat facts the birth needs, and reading them first would mint a second defeat read) — then WC's own `brigand` tag event on a `free_unit` (`peopleLedger.js :: TAG_EVENTS`), so the deserter gang IS that free unit in WC's `raiding` state, its counts conserved by WC's ledger; CH's corps failure rung `desertion` ("its strength band falls one, the shed blocks taking WC's own dissolution road") feeds the same road | sized to the loss (the strength the loser shed, banded; DRAFT `thin`–`modest`) | the nearest cover to the rout | "Formed from the deserters of Kell's levy, a gang holds the Tolle hills." |
| (e) FROM MIGRANTS — `cast_out` | gang | a migrant flow that could not be placed (`demographicsMigration.js :: MIGRATION_REFUSALS` `unreachable` · `no_capacity`, the landed placement outcomes), REFUSED at the wall (POP-1's `column_at_the_wall` `refused` · `dispersed`, when it lands), turned away at a CLOSED gate for people (plague-R7's stance slot — when it lands), STRANDED by a slowed pass or a bad season (the climate band slot), or PREYED ON by a gang on the road (§9.6) | sized to the group (`thin`; `modest` from a great flight, DRAFT) | the nearest cover to where it was turned away | "Formed from the Tollish migrants turned away at Kell's gate." |

So CLOSING THE GATES IS NOT FREE (a counterforce on the people's gate stance, when that stance lands), and a group LET IN with no work swells the town's quarter instead — it raises the crime pressure the town's own spill reads (birth (a) by another road), never a second body. The migrant group itself is never an actor (a flow); the gang is. A spill takes nothing from the town's criminal floor: the town's criminal power is the corruption plane's (`computeGuildStrengthBy` derives it from captured factions; BD writes no faction), so a spill takes nothing from the town's floor; the new gang starts with a SMALL standing in its home town (its kin and fences there, DRAFT 0.1) and earns the rest by its acts — the town's crime is the floor, the gang's grip the composition above it (§7.3). A body is a BAND, never a headcount, and no census or army count is debited by a birth (macro over micro; OD-PR-32's precedent). Every body is born NAMELESS unless born at or above its kind's NAMING BAND (a metropolis quarter's `capable` spill is named at birth: "the Lantern Street gang"); a beast is never named (BD23).

### 3.3 The dwell
Each source has its OWN dwell clock (`births['<word>:<placeKey>']`, DRAFT 13 weeks for (a) and (d), 26 for a mission, a season for (c) and `bred`): a source that bore a body bears no other until its dwell has passed; and NO birth in a country in the week a body there died (BD7: "never magically, never the same week"). The dwell is what makes killing a body buy a season of quiet.

### 3.4 The cap
ONE CAP per country: at most `BODY_CAP_PER_COUNTRY` bodies whose `where` or base lies in T's country (DRAFT 2; 3 in `plagued` country), and a world cap (DRAFT one body per two settlements, min 3) for cost; a birth that would exceed either is not drawn (receipted only on scrutiny: "the hills hold all the gangs they can feed"). The cap and the dwell are the volume's two runaway guards beside supplies (§6). The number is never computed as a target: births are drawn against the balance and ends follow from supplies and fights, so the count EMERGES from the balance (unpredictable in prospect, legible on scrutiny) and the cap only bounds it. The cap is also a GEOMETRY — two gangs' operating radii (§5.4) PARTITION a region's roads, and where they OVERLAP is the feud (the `feud` choice's weight is the shared stretches of their radii, §4.2; a meeting there is `bodies_meet`, §9.1); a SPONSORED body may hold a SIZE its habitat could not feed (the sponsor's flow is supplies, §10.4 — bounded by the sponsor's purse), but never a place past the count cap.

### 3.5 Clock, tier, counterforce, felt
- **Clock:** weekly evaluation; dwell per source (§3.3); births are rare (DRAFT: in a typical canonized world of fifteen towns, a handful of bodies alive at a time, UNMEASURED — BD-1's experience read measures it).
- **Tier:** gangs and beasts SPATIAL CANON (they live on the land; embattlement is canon-only); a conclave's mission birth needs `faithSpreadEnabled` (the spread lane: dramatic and full) and the canon for the road (aspatially it hops the relationship graph at `UNPRICED_LEG_WEEKS`, CH's fallback); a `driven_out` flock is aspatial-safe (it leaves to the friendliest neighbour by graph).
- **Counterforce:** security (§2.5), the cap, the dwell; for missions the target court's stance (§7.1).
- **Receipt and felt sentence:** the birthplace receipt is the Herald line, named for WHO (the body), WHICH POWER (its source), WHAT EFFECT (where it now is); no forecast ("a gang has taken to the hill roads", never "a gang will raid").
- **Surface · scrutiny · machinery:** surface — one Herald line at birth (never pushed unless the country is pinned) and the map piece behind the toggle; scrutiny — the birth card (source, size, the habitat parts, the security parts); machinery — the draw and its odds.

## §4 THE WEEKLY CHOICE — the house idiom: a bounded choice drawn by the seeded stream from a situation-scored set (BD-1, BD-3; the owner's ~18:1x "gather forces where it is feasible and RNG weighted rolls on situtation", ~18:5x "how conclaves react depend on their faith's alignment and the personality of their diety combined")

### 4.1 The idiom
For every unpinned body (and every creed's `cult` row, §4.2) each week: `bodyChoiceSet` scores each choice of its kind's closed set — `score(c) = BASE[bodyKind][c] × Π factor(c)`, each factor a BOUNDED multiplier `[0.25, 4]` with a REASON (the `{ mult, reason }` idiom of `STRESSOR_SPAWN_GATES`), every BASE and factor DRAFT, UNMEASURED; ONE pick by `weightedPick` on the stream `bodies:<bodyId>:<tick>` (the pulse's PRNG forked by a stable key — consumption-independent, so no other stream moves; the HBF row `bodyChoicePick`, BD-1's commit). The receipt keeps the pick and its top three reasons (`record.choice`) — legible on scrutiny, never a forecast on the surface. A CHAOTIC god's flock and a chaotic-led gang read the widest weights (the factors flattened toward 1: unpredictable in prospect, the owner's Dwarf Fortress standard). The choice DECIDES THE ACT; the act's writer is always the landed one (BD5). FOUR more factors enter the same product, each bounded `[0.25, 4]` with its reason — (i) a NAMED body with a LEADER reads the leader's recorded axes (CH's chart, `characterAxes`) in place of its kind's defaults (a cautious captain weights `move` and the private target; a reckless one the public target and the `feud`); (ii) its LESSONS, `bodyHabitTilt` (§4.4), never deciding alone; (iii) a SPONSOR's purpose (§10.4) as ONE weight toward the purpose's acts (a sponsored gang leans to the rival's roads, a sponsored flock to `supplicate` in the rival's town) scaled by its trust of the sponsor — the body keeps its mind; (iv) a target's VALUE × VISIBILITY against its RETALIATION risk (§9.5): a publicly contracted caravan and an envoy on a public mission weigh more as prey and cost more when struck, read by the body's personality (its leader's, else its god's for a flock, else its kind's).

### 4.2 The three sets and what each reads

| Kind | Choice | What raises it (the factor's landed read; each with its reason) | What the act does (through whose writer) |
|---|---|---|---|
| conclave | `rally` | low cohesion; a court stance turning against it; a rival flock or a gang near (fear) | +cohesion; the creed's strength in its town this week ×(1 + ε) through `strengthByRef` (§7.4) |
| conclave | `preach` | high local piety; a fractured old faith; a court that `permitted` or `endowed` it; a peaceful (`peacelike`) or lawful god | the creed's strength lean at its town (the faith system's share law moves the share, `SHARE_STEP_MAX` 6); in a town where the creed is absent, `attemptEntry` at the faith system's own bar |
| conclave | `supplicate` | a LAWFUL god (`chaos01` low) — "petitions the court first" (the owner, item 13); a court stance `curbed`; a held shrine wanted | mints `body_stance` at the town's court (§12): the court's fork answers (§7.1) |
| conclave | `hide` | `outlawed` or `curbed` by a hunting court; a gang or an army that sacks near | in town: WF-5b's covert congregation (`.deities[ref].covert`, "in place, never a parallel ledger") when WF-5b is lit — else the row's ordinary life with the stance's strength lean; on the move: the hidden ways (§5.3); at a site: ST's `keptSecret` (court knowledge only) |
| conclave | `defy` | a CHAOTIC or `warlike` god; strong local followers under an `outlawed` stance | practises openly: the creed's strength rises (its followers rally) AND the court's legitimacy among them falls and their unrest rises (§7.5); the court's next `body_stance` fork reads it |
| conclave | `move` | `driven_out`; a mission's goal; the cap reached at home; a friendlier neighbour by believed stance | leaves (becomes or stays a body), or enters a town by `attemptEntry` and ends `dissolved` |
| conclave | `confront` | a GANG that terrorizes the flock's people (a `raid` `succeeds` or a `traffic_waylaid` on the towns or the stretches where its followers live, this season) and the god's `stanceOf(god, the villain's axes)` AGGRESSION (`AGG_GOOD_VS_EVIL`); a `warlike` temper raises it; the flock believes it can meet the gang at its size — the gang's might band as its creed's seat holds it (`believedBodyOf` there, BD12) — (else it shelters, `parleyed`) | SEEKS the meeting on §9.1's SEEK term (the owner, ~18:5x: "a good natured upstanding conclave … would more likley refuse, pursue, or fight a brigand that is terrorizing the people"): its stretch is the gang's last believed place; met, `bodies_meet` defaults `fought`; a creed's `cult` ROW in town has no `where` and no might (BD3), so ITS `confront` is the in-town REFUSAL AND WARNING — it refuses the gang's demand and sends word to the watch (an `informer` sighting row of the gang in its court's store, §2.4 (iii)) — and only a MOVING flock (a body) SEEKS the meeting |
| conclave | `raid` | ONLY toward a court that holds the flock `curbed` or `outlawed` (hostile PER COURT — BD14): `strained`/`starving` supplies; a rural settlement OF SUCH A COURT, or a caravan or migrants on such a court's land, within its radius it BELIEVES it outmatches — its might band against the target's VISIBLE walls band (`bodyVisibleDefenceOf`, BD12; the producer's BD14 threshold decides the act); weighted by its god's axes — an evil or warlike god's flock readily (`COOP_EVIL_TRANSACTIONAL`, the warlike temper), a good one only `starving` and hunted (a factor DRAFT ×0.25, never zero), never against a court that permits or endows it | the registry's `raid` with the flock the raider (§9.2): supplies and bodies, a captive only where its god allows (§9.10) |
| gang | `raid` | `strained` or `starving` supplies (a raid is a supply act first, BD9); a rural settlement, a caravan, migrants or a patrol within its radius it BELIEVES it outmatches — a settlement by its VISIBLE walls band (`bodyVisibleDefenceOf`), a caravan or migrants by the size its ties have heard, a patrol by `believedWatch` — the PRODUCER's BD14 threshold and the band contest deciding the act (BD12); a lawless home | the target's kind (§9.2 `raid`, §9.6, §9.3) |
| gang | `smuggle` | a gate town inside its radius where its standing or the guild is strong; trade through that gate | its standing at the gate town rises (§7.3) — the smuggling gate reads the composed guild through `buildSmuggleContext` (one smuggling law, EC/TR's) |
| gang | `bribe` | a watch whose wages go unpaid (THE PATROLS' item 16 slot: PR's riders unpaid, `wantSince` on a keep); a tolerant town (`effectiveToleranceOf` disorder high) | the court's stance toward it moves one step toward `tolerated` at that court's next `body_stance` fork (a court's act, never the gang's write) and its standing there rises |
| gang | `recruit` | `provisioned`; habitat (unrest, famine, the levy away, cast-out migrants near) | might up one band at the next upkeep if supplies hold (§6.2) |
| gang | `feud` | a `rival` or `hostile` gang inside its radius it BELIEVES it outmatches — the rival's might band as its HOME town holds it (`believedBodyOf(worldState, homeId, rivalId).mightBand`, BD12's ties), else the pair's last `fought` incident (§7.2) | seeks the meeting (§9.1) |
| gang | `move` | low cohesion and no base (seek an empty den: ST's `site_taken`); hunted where it stands; the radius dry of prey | base change (ST's statuses) or roaming |
| gang | `scatter` | `starving`, beaten, hunted with no trail it believes unknown | ends `scattered` this week (a receipt) — part of its standing remains as the town's floor's ordinary crime (no write: the composition simply loses its term) |
| beast | `prowl` | hunger (supplies below `provisioned`); kills inside its radius (livestock — EC's herds read; travellers; migrants; a patrol) weighed by HUNGER, SEASON and COVER alone (BD12: a beast plans on nothing else; whether a strike on a settlement or a patrol LANDS is the producer's threshold and the encounter's truth); WINTER (hunger drives the pack to the roads) | kills: its supply rises; the target's harm through the landed reads (§9.6, §9.2) |
| beast | `roam` | the radius dry; hunting pressure; summer | shifts its territory (its lair stays its base unless it `flee`s) |
| beast | `lie_up` | `provisioned`; spring (breeding); wounds (a lost fight) | rests at its lair; `bred` in spring (§3.2) |
| beast | `flee` | hunted at a size it cannot meet; its lair cleared | leaves the region for the most-covered neighbour; its lair is `abandoned` by ST's writer (a body's own move, ST §6.1 step 1; ST7: "empty = no effect"), a den a gang may take |

**THE GOD'S CHARACTER (the owner's item 13) enters every conclave factor ONCE through `stanceOf(the conclave's god, X)`** where X is the god or the actor in question: toward a GANG, X is the villain's axes (`alignmentAxis: 'evil'`, `lawAxis: 'chaotic'` — "a gang's 'alignment' is its DEEDS (villainous by nature)", the owner's §8 inherent value), so a GOOD god's flock reads `aggression` toward the gang (`AGG_GOOD_VS_EVIL` 0.55: refuse its extortion, warn the watch, shelter its victims, fight it at its size — the `confront` choice that SEEKS it) and an EVIL god's flock reads `cooperation` (`COOP_EVIL_TRANSACTIONAL`: it extorts beside the gang, hires it, trades with it); the DERIVED temper (`deityTemper`) splits the good: `warlike` fights, `peacelike` shelters. A LAWFUL god weights `supplicate` first; a CHAOTIC one flattens every weight. The conclave→gang stance prior is by ALIGNMENT, never by kind (the owner's item 13), built on the ONE landed stance function, so no BD table of alignment pairs exists.

### 4.3 Clock, tier, counterforce, felt
- **Clock:** weekly, one choice per body; the choice is a receipt, not a commitment (next week's choice reads next week's situation); `move` toward a base is sticky by cohesion (a body with a base weights staying).
- **Tier:** the gang and beast sets canon-only; the conclave's in-town choice aspatial-safe (it reads the religion row and the court stance only); every preset where its flag is lit; the OMNISCIENT PRESET's arm is the same (the choice's belief reads — `believedStanceOf`, `believedBodyOf`, `believedWatch` — read truth in the omniscient arm; the VISIBLE walls band is the same public face in every preset) (the BELIEF preset is the default once C0 lights).
- **Counterforce:** each choice's own price — a raid risks the band contest and a hunt; `defy` costs legitimacy among followers and invites `outlawed`; `bribe` costs supplies; `recruit` costs supplies; `scatter` ends the body.
- **Felt:** "The Grey Lady's faithful in Kell refused the hill gang's demand and sent word to the watch." · "The Tolle gang has fallen on Low Ford for its grain."
- **Surface · scrutiny · machinery:** surface — only the ACT's line (when an act happens); scrutiny — the choice and its three reasons on the body's card; machinery — the scores and the draw.

### 4.4 HABITS — one law, three grains, two learners (BD24; the owner, 18:30: "don't forget the habits formed by the settlement, its patrols, and actors in response to beasts, conclaves, and gangs and those latter groups (indiidual to the specific group/body) response as well to each other and the patrol in their pursuit and evasion.")
- **The substrate, measured (`cure/D-BD-r4.md` m4):** HB's ONE ledger `spatialLedgers.habits` — "`rows: { <actorId>: { <class>: { <action>: [stockInt, weekStamp] } } }`", its writer refusing only "`if (!actorId || !action || !isCircumstanceClass(className)) return;`", the class "an ARGUMENT", "`HABIT_ROWS_PER_ACTOR_CAP: 24`", HB's curve `habitCurve.js` (`applyCredit`; the decay and the tilt cap HB's), stamped on `calendar.elapsedWeeks` (HB's clock law), gated by `habitConditioningEnabled` (lit nowhere). BD writes NO second record: a body id IS an actor id there, and BD supplies only its closed CLASSIFIER and its ACTION KEYS.
- **(c) EACH BODY, INDIVIDUALLY** (never its kind; a beast too — it learns where the hunters ride): three of HB's twelve landed classes carry a body's lessons in their landed meanings — `route_menaced` (its way was threatened: the ACTION KEY is the stretch or trail it took, `stretch:<a|b>` / `trail:<a|b>`; credited UP when it slipped through, DOWN where it was MET — "avoid where caught"), `stores_thin` (its supplies were strained: the key is the target it struck or the town it asked — up where the take or the alms were good, "return where prey was easy, where alms were given"), `rival_ascendant` (another body pressed it: the key is that body's id — down where it lost, so it avoids what beat it, and a feud hardens where it won). The RIDERS' CADENCE is not a body row: it is THE PATROLS' host fact (PR's round, a cadence a body can learn), read by the body's PLANNING only through `believedWatch` (belief, never the roster — PR §8.3; ROUTE PR: the cadence as a learnable fact). The DRAFT credit sizes are HB's (UNMEASURED); a body's rows DROP when it ends — through ONE `ends` change form BD-10 adds to HB's ONE writer `writeHabits` (`changes.ends: [actorId]`; `habitLedger.js` 134 eff, +≈4 lines; its caller list THE BODIES' stage alone; `pruneRows` drops only neutral stocks, so without it an ended gang's rows survive — EXECUTED, `hbGhost.out`) — and are HALVED when its LEADER changes (L-BD 39's "a new captain … resets a gang's habits" read on the landed SEAT SHOCK: `habitCurve.js :: seatAdjusted`, "a partial keep, never a reset", `SEAT_SHOCK_KEEP` 0.5 — HB's seat rule at the body grain, cited honestly), THE ENTRY SEAT RESOLVED WITHOUT A STORED SEAT (HB's rows are "`[stockInt, weekStamp]`" and `seatAdjusted` says "An unresolvable seat identity applies NO shock"): the CURRENT seat is `p:<wnpcId>` of `bodyLeaderOf`, else `faceless:<bodyId>` (never empty, so a FIRST leader halves the lessons as a later one does); a row whose `weekStamp` is EARLIER than CH's `paragon.leads.sinceTick` predates the current leader and reads its ENTRY seat as `prior:<bodyId>`, else the current one — `seatAdjusted(stock, entry, current)` on read, baked into the stock by HB's `applyCredit` at the row's first credit after the change; a leader LOST with no successor halves nothing (the band keeps what it learned; its cohesion falls, §6.4).
- **(a) THE TOWN AND ITS COURT — BD SUPPLIES THE CLASSIFIER'S ARGUMENT ITSELF** (BD-10's classifier hands it one of THREE LANDED classes in their landed meanings — no HB row, no thirteenth class (`HABIT_CLASS_CEILING` 12 is owner-signed)): the court's stance PRIOR toward a FLOCK under `faith_contested` (HB: "schism, conversion pressure, or a tradition claim is live"; the ACTION KEY the stance word it took — `curbed` · `outlawed` · `permitted` · `endowed` — credited up where the town's fracture eased after it, down where it rose); toward a GANG, and its BOUNTY, under `route_menaced` (HB: "a route this actor depends on carries live embattlement or toll pressure"; the keys `hunted` · `tolerated` · `employed` and `posted` · `withheld` — up where its roads quieted after it, down where they did not); §7.1's fork takes ONE habit term from them (a court that has curbed flocks drifts toward curbing the next); its GATE stance toward migrants under `crowding_pressed` (HB: "the demographic overflow band is `pressed` or `overflowing`"; a town that bred a gang from refused migrants learns either way) — ⛔ DARK until plague-R7's gate stance lands (the slot); the court's actor id is HB's own settlement key; the court's paragons as CH already gives them.
- **(b) THE PATROL** (PR's text; ROUTE PR): its ROUND is a habit (a predictable cadence — the map's cadence words made a host fact a body can learn), its grouping and pursuit too; the riders' PREDICTION reads THIS body's rows (PR §5.5: "THIS body's own habits where HB carries the body grain … else its kind's") — `BODY_HABITS` (§9.3) is the KIND's prior, the fallback when a body has no rows.
- **THE BALANCE IS THE PRICE:** a habitual gang is predictable and caught; a habitual patrol is predictable and evaded. VARIATION is the lever: the seeded situation roll BOUNDS every habit factor (`bodyHabitTilt` ∈ `[0.75, 1.33]`, DRAFT, UNMEASURED — a habit never decides alone); the DM's `PATROL_ORDER` breaks a round's rut; a new captain halves a gang's lessons (HB's seat shock).
- **Clock · tier · felt:** weekly credit (HB's); `habitConditioningEnabled` ∧ `bodiesEnabled` (dark: every tilt is 1 — byte-identical); "The Hollow Road gang has learned Kell's riders come on the fourth week" (scrutiny) · "Kell's riders now ride the hill road first" (surface).

---

## §5 HOW BODIES MOVE — the one physics, the hidden ways, the body's choice of road or way, the radius (BD-5; the owner's ~18:3x "they don't travel the overt roads … maybe hidden roads that connect between roads, and multiple of them", ~19:3x "sites … acts as a home base where they maintain something of a bounded radius to in distance")

### 5.1 The one physics
A body moves on the ONE physics: WY's roads (the digest's primary hops), the season's cost (`seasonalPathCost`, SLOW NOT SEVER — winter slows a pass, never cuts it), the climate band when it lands (the slot), priced by `distanceRead.js :: hopWeeks`. It NEVER asks passage (PS1). It is a node-keyed WHERE (BD20) that advances at most one hop per week (DRAFT; a body is slower than a column on the road and faster than nothing).

### 5.2 THE HIDDEN WAYS — the lowest rungs of the ONE road ladder (`hiddenWayEdges`, BD-5)
- **ONE LADDER, MASTER'S RUNG.** Master's route ladder ALREADY holds the hidden rung, its access law, its price and its life — `routeNetworkLedger.js :: ROUTE_GRADES` "`['highway', 'road', 'track', 'hidden']`"; `routeNetworkConsumers.js :: HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS` "`['covert_envoy', 'smuggler', 'wanderer']`" behind `mayUseHiddenPaths`, which "fails CLOSED"; `routeNetworkConsumersTransit.js`'s "`hidden: 2`" "PINNED" equal to `npcConsequencesTuning.js`'s "`HIDDEN_PATH_SLOWDOWN: 2,`" ("wanderers may use hidden paths (slowly); armies may not"); `routeNetworkDecayLifecycle.js`'s "A dead town's roads go HIDDEN" (dark behind the virtual `routeLifecycleEnabled`; `cure/D-BD-r5.md` m3). THE BODIES' ways ARE that rung — in the map contract's words the rungs `wild · way` (the host's `hidden` CLASS) and, worn by use, `trail`, below `track · road · highway` — and NEVER a second network: (a) ACCESS — ONE new TRAVELLER token, `body`, enters BOTH of master's closed arrays, `TRAVELLER_KINDS` and `HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS`, codepoint-sorted (master's walker "`for (const kind of HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS) expect(TRAVELLER_KINDS).toContain(kind);`" and its exact pin "`expect([...TRAVELLER_KINDS]).toEqual(['army', 'caravan', 'covert_envoy', 'smuggler', 'wanderer'])`" RE-RECORDED with its cause at BD-5 — the ES-1 precedent), a DECLARED change on a landed access law (a spy is the landed `covert_envoy`, a smuggler the landed `smuggler`); NO OTHER TRAVELLER TOKEN is added — PR's `patrol` token enters neither array: a patrol or a warden-led detachment on a way ITS HOLDER KNOWS is the holder's own unit and passes by the holder's knowledge read (`knownTrailsOf`, §2.3), never by a traveller token; "armies may not" STANDS for every other unit, widened for a military unit that KNOWS a way only by THE PASSAGE's ONE declared change (PS-4's `travellerMayWalk(kind, hop, { might })` — PS the widening's ONE owner, WC's chooser its wartime reader on the owner's 22:51 word, L-BD 11i; its peace and war scope PS's own text); (b) PRICE — one price per way: the landed `gradeLegMultiplier('hidden')` 2 (= `HIDDEN_PATH_SLOWDOWN` 2) for EVERY unit — persons, bodies, smugglers, spies, detachments, a small unit — EXCEPT BEASTS, ×1 (the owner, 22:19: "traveling on a hidden path for all units is slower, except for beasts"; cover is their ground); the season's overlay on both networks, slow-not-sever (a winter way slows, never cuts); no second trail price exists anywhere (the LANDED constant wins); a change to the factor is a cost-law version bump and the owner's tuning (THE PROMISE); (c) LIFE — the route lifecycle's own state machine moves each way's rung — the way-mode edge's `wayRung`, never `gradeRank` (the road decay's "`if (fresh && gradeRank(from) < gradeRank('highway'))`" would promote a hidden-grade edge to `track`, so the decay and the charter bypass SKIP mode `way` — WY's half) (BD20): USE raises it (the map contract: "Each use counts. After enough use the life program raises the path's status: wild, then a way, then a trail"), a town's ADOPTION to a track, growth to a chartered road between the way's two junctions (WY's adoption), DISUSE lowers it step by step and a road is never erased; a NEW way FORMS where bodies keep crossing the same cover corridor (a desire path: the candidate becomes usable after the lifecycle's use threshold, DRAFT, UNMEASURED); BD's ONE input is the way-mode edge's `useCount` its bodies' crossings add (§2.7 step 5; a field on the edge, decayed on the lifecycle's clock, never a flow class; the lifecycle's own write). CONSEQUENCES by construction (never designed separately; 11f): a pack cannot be run down on a way — it is AMBUSHED at its den or at a junction; a gang on a way is exactly as slow as the riders behind it, so pursuit of a gang is a PREDICTION and HABITS game, never a race; a spy by the quiet way arrives later than one by the road. With `routeLifecycleEnabled` dark the ways are the CANDIDATES alone at the hidden price (no rung moves — the DEGRADED ARM; the map draws them `wild`). The access token is `body` (one kind, its `bodyKind` a field), never three tokens.
- **THE CANDIDATE GEOMETRY — derived, buildable now; the map's when it lands.** The map draws "every plausible footpath through the rough country, forest, hills, marsh, mountains, but marks them all 'wild' … On open plain there are none" (the contract); until it does, the host DERIVES the same candidates deterministically: for every primary hop `a|b` the digest carries a frozen terrain receipt (`routeReceipts[…].byTerrain`, "per primary hop"), its COVER SHARE `cover01 = Σ byTerrain[forest, wetland, mountain] / Σ byTerrain`, and (i) a corridor `trail:<a|b>` BESIDE the road where `cover01 ≥ TRAIL_COVER_FLOOR` (DRAFT 0.40) — lit, the way-mode edge `routeEdgeId(a, b, 'way')` beside the road's `land` edge; (ii) a BYPASS corridor keyed by the node it SKIRTS, `trail:<a|c>~<b>` (never `trail:<a|c>`, which a corridor beside a road `a|c` or a bypass round another node would share), BETWEEN DIFFERENT road nodes for every node `b` whose two neighbours `a` and `c` both reach it through cover at or above the floor — a way that skirts `b` (its gate, its toll, its riders), its cover the lesser of the two hops', its length their sum, its JUNCTIONS the `a`-side and `c`-side stretches (lit, its way-mode edge runs JUNCTION TO JUNCTION — `junction:<a|b>@<b>` to `junction:<b|c>@<b>`, the points on the two stretches nearest `b`, each a point on ONE primary hop by the junction rule, the map's own `junction:<id>` replacing each when it names it — so its identity never collides with a settlement pair's; for a UNIT's hops, PS's `hiddenWayFor`, a bypass reads as the primary hops `a|b` and `b|c` with `b`'s GATE and TOLL never entered: the territory crossed is `b`'s LAND, PS's quiet-crossing rule at `b`'s border deciding whether it is witnessed; a meeting on it takes the WHERE `road (a, b)` or `road (b, c)` of the nearer junction's stretch) — so a way CONNECTS BETWEEN ROADS and a rough corridor holds MORE THAN ONE way (the owner, ~18:3x: "hidden roads that connect between roads, and multiple of them") — PLAUSIBLE until BD-5's car-0 measures the bypass count on a seed family; the map's canon supersedes it. NONE across open plain, desert, tundra or glacier (there a body must cross the overt road and be seen). The brief's cover classes "forest · hills · mountain · marsh" are not the code's: the landed `TERRAIN_CLASSES` carry no `hills` (elevation is an overlay that reads `mountain` above `MOUNTAIN_HEIGHT` 60) and call marsh `wetland`; cover is `forest · wetland · mountain`. The candidate geometry is never stored; a lit way is the route ledger's edge in its own mode `way` (BD20).
- **Capacity:** a way carries at most a `modest` gang or flock (DRAFT; "a body of thirty, never a column"); a `capable` or larger one is too many to hide and must take the road (a BEAST pack is EXEMPT — it ranges the cover itself, never hides as a column would) — so a gang that grows past the ways' capacity is forced into the open (a counterforce on recruitment). A SMALL military unit may take one too (PS's quiet crossing: slower, size-capped, witnessed only on a known and watched way — CARRIED at PS8 · PS §5.6; in WAR a unit weighs a way it KNOWS in its ONE risk read — knowledge, capacity (a column SPLITS into band-sized parts and pays in cohesion and time), speed and attrition, the GAIN (SURPRISE: out of a way the enemy does not know, the defender fights unmustered — a bounded lean on WC's contest's defender term; ESCAPE; PURSUIT at the way's pace), the RISK (a way the enemy knows and watches turns the ambush around at the junction) — WC's chooser and the generals design's leader weigh it, PS's crossing rule unchanged; L-BD 11i, ROUTE WC · PS · PR); a unit's size is read on the SAME might ladder as a body's (`thin · modest · capable · strong · formidable`, PS §2.1 · §5.6), so "a body of thirty, never a column" is one line for both (L-BD 41 (a): a warden-led detachment rides a way only within this capacity, PR §5.5). Caravans never take a way. A SMUGGLER does — the landed `smuggler` franchise (`HIDDEN_PATH_KINDS`): EC's gauntlet run moves onto a way when the smuggler CHOOSES it (the same choice roll as BD11 — the gate's stance `closed · taxed · restricted` and plague-R7's quarantine, the believed coverage on the road, against the way's time and capacity), is caught only on a KNOWN and WATCHED way (PR's trail arm), and a run that passes costs the holder its TOLL and the gate its stance's effect (the holder's own reason to learn the ways); a sponsor's courier (§10.4 (b)) is the same run with silver as the contraband; a smuggler is never a body (a flow or a named person on ES's spine), so no stance and no hearing of its own — the seizure is the gauntlet's own outcome, receipted on the way (the owner, 22:14: "smugglers can also travel hidden paths as well to avoid patrols and gates") (L-BD 11b; ROUTE EC — the gauntlet's path choice).
- **Junctions:** where a way meets a road is a JUNCTION — "where ambushes happen and where patrols set their checkpoints. Junctions have names" (the contract); a corridor beside `a|b` meets its own stretch, a bypass meets the stretches at its two ends, and once the map names a junction its key is `junction:<id>` beside `stretch:<a|b>` under the card's WHERE `road (a, b)` (never a sixth WHERE kind; the fold registers it). A gang SORTIES from the way onto the road (it stands on the road's half that week, where the riders may MEET it — PR §8.1's truth) and returns. The BELIEVED junctions (the ways a town KNOWS, §2.3) are the patrols' choke points: PR's round plans by them (ROUTE PR — PR cites, one read). By the owner's junction rule a chartered road may start or end at a junction on another road (WY's).
- **Belief:** a way is KNOWN or UNKNOWN per town (§2.3), wholly or as a prefix; a patrol's coverage on an UNKNOWN way is ZERO; on a known WATCHED way it is the round's coverage at the junctions (PR's one coverage read). A town learns a way by six sources (§2.3: a catch, a survey, a guide, a BROKER, a PURSUIT, the DM). "The watch has learned the smugglers' path" is the felt sign.
- **PLAGUE ON THE WAYS (a law; the owner's word of 18:27 on L-BD 37):** a gate CLOSED for quarantine (plague-R7's stance slot — the host's own verb, when built) stops the ROAD, never the HIDDEN WAY: a body that crosses from an afflicted country by a way carries the plague past the gate the town shut. A body is a TRAVELLER to THE PLAGUE's one truth — its arrival in a town's country from an afflicted one is ONE seeding through PG's own door (pg-1's column seeding into the epidemic ledger, counted against its per-tick `COLUMN_SEED_CAP`; PG: "Seeding lands in the epidemic ledger, never a second system") — never a second plague law, never a spread channel of BD's own; DARK until pg-1 lands (the slot); ROUTE PG (a bracket on the quarantine slot: "the road shut, not the way"). Measured: `cure/D-BD-r4.md` m11.
- **THE COVER IS ON THE DIGEST (L-BD 38's risk, settled by measurement):** the derived candidate geometry needs terrain BETWEEN road nodes, and the digest carries it per primary hop (`routeReceipts[…].byTerrain`, §0.1) — the hidden ways are BUILDABLE NOW on the host's derivation; the map's per-stretch ground classes (the host document's ENC-HP-09, "needs T2") sharpen it and the map's own hidden-ways layer (ENC-HL-12, MISSING; the ask `refs/PROMPT-FOR-THE-MAPMAKER-HIDDEN-WAYS-2026-10-18.md`) overrides it when it lands — the two-layer shape, the canon half first where it exists.
- **PIRATES ARE GANGS ON THE LANES (L-BD 45):** where the sea lanes are lit (TRADE M8's lanes; `seaRoadsEnabled` for the roads missions' sea arm), a gang whose base is a COVE — ST's `den` kind on a coast, no new site kind — works the LANES as a land gang works the roads: its presence row stands on the lane's hops (the one physics: lanes are roads in every read), the lane's own losses fire through the landed piracy parity (`banditryLoss`/`scoreRoute` on lanes; the roads gauntlet's S3 piracy arm, frozen — attributed, never edited), and the patrols' and the navy's answers are the landed ones (W11's blockade is a navy's, never a gang's). No second piracy law.
- **Beasts take both — the way at their own pace, the road as the easier ground**: a pack RANGES country to country over cover and its ways at ×1 (cover is its ground; the landed hidden price binds every other unit), and comes down onto the ROAD's half when the road is the easier ground — winter hunger above all ("winter brings the pack to the roads", §6.3) — where the riders may meet it (PR §8.1). Its choice is BD11's roll like any body's (hunger · the season · cover · the hunters it has learned, §4.4); a beast needs no access token beyond `body` (its kind is a field).
- **The map's canon overrides — THE CONTRACT (`refs/MAP-REPLY-HIDDEN-WAYS-2026-10-18.md`, adopted 22:52):** the map draws the land's every plausible footpath at birth at `wild`, names its junctions and draws each way at its current rung; the host sends the facts ("town A now knows this path", "this path is now a trail") through the window, never invents geography; the host's derivation is the fallback until the map's layer lands, the canon the authority (the site record's two-layer shape). The remaining asks (T2 per-stretch ground classes; the width/kind hint; the milestone; the size budget) carry to the next exchange (ROUTE FOLD — the map INBOX). Drawn on the map behind the bodies toggle ONLY once a town the audience may see KNOWS the way; the DM sees the ghosts of dead roads (the DM's tool).

### 5.3 Road or way — the body chooses each move (BD11)
Each week a moving body plans its next hop and CHOOSES the network for it — `bodyRouteChoice` (§2.5), ONE keyed draw on the stream `bodies:route:<bodyId>:<tick>` over the two (or more) priced options, road and each way it knows within capacity, `score(option) = BASE × Π factor` with every factor bounded `[0.25, 4]` and its reason (§4.1's idiom; DRAFT, UNMEASURED): (1) the STANCE PRIOR — what it BELIEVES the holder of each land thinks of it (`believedStanceOf`): `permitted · endowed · tolerated · employed · harboured` weight the road, `curbed · outlawed · hunted` the way — a weight, NEVER a prohibition; (2) BELIEVED COVERAGE on each — the road's from `believedWatch` (PR §8.3, "the same read ES/CH/PT use"), a way's ZERO where it believes the town does not know it and the round's where it believes the junction watched (a body that has heard a town's public-security office is held by a WARDEN believes that office's ways WATCHED — deterrence by belief, PR §5.5 (b)); (3) CAPACITY — a way above its band is no option (§5.2); (4) COST — the way's landed ×2 (×1 for a beast) against the road's, the season on both; (5) URGENCY — a pursued body weights the faster escape, a starving one the shorter road to prey; (6) HABITS — `bodyHabitTilt` on `route_menaced` (§4.4: away from where it was caught); (7) WHAT THE WAY PASSES AROUND — an ARMY on the march on the road (PS12's enemy-column presence read as a danger weight), the territory holder's TOLL, the GATE's stance and its question (PS's ladder; plague-R7's quarantine) — the owner's "to avoid military and armies and tolls and gates". The receipt names the choice and its top reason ("the Hollow Road gang risked the Tolle road: the drover's way was watched"; "a tolerated flock took the goat path round Merewick's toll"). No option at all (no road it dares, no way it knows) → it waits, turns, or crosses the road at the least-watched believed half. A body that is HOSTILE by stance plans EVERY move to avoid patrols (the owner, ~19:0x: "hostile gangs, conclaves, and beasts are trying to avoid patrols") — the coverage weight carries it. A body sees ONE BANNER in a kingdom: the believed coverage composes the kingdom's riders by what the body has heard (THE BANNER — deterrence by belief, never the ownership). PR's encounter applies on EITHER network (coverage × cover; a way's coverage zero unless known, §5.2).

### 5.4 The base and the radius (BD9)
A body with a HELD SITE (ST's holder ref names it) operates within a BOUNDED RADIUS in weeks of travel from that site (`BODY_RADIUS_WEEKS[bodyKind][might]`, DRAFT: a `thin` gang two weeks, a `modest` three, a conclave one week from its shrine, a beast its territory of two weeks), priced by `hopWeeks` on the road and the ways it would choose (§5.3: the cheaper option it believes it can take; the ways at the landed ×2, a beast's at ×1); it raids, hunts, recruits and meets only inside it. MOVING THE BASE is the `move` choice (a new `site_taken` or an `abandoned`, ST's statuses; never a drift) — the new base written only when ST's closing call returns `arrived` (§2.7 step 5). A body WITHOUT a site is ROAMING: its radius is one week around where it stands and its cohesion decays fast (BD9) — the pressure to take an empty den. A beast's lair and its territory are the same shape.

### 5.5 Clock, tier, counterforce, felt
- **Clock:** a hop per week at most; a town's knowledge of a way FADES on HB's curve unless used, watched or refreshed (§2.3, L-BD 11f — no fixed term in weeks); a way's RUNG moves on the route lifecycle's own clock (BD20).
- **Tier:** SPATIAL CANON (the digest's receipts); `hiddenWaysEnabled` for the ways (dark: every body takes the road and the radius is priced on roads alone — the DEGRADED ARM); `routeLifecycleEnabled` for a way's RUNG to move (dark: the candidates alone at the hidden price, no use counter written); aspatially a conclave hops the relationship graph (`UNPRICED_LEG_WEEKS`).
- **Counterforce:** the trails' capacity; known and watched junctions; the hunt (§9.3); the radius (a body far from its base is weak and short-lived).
- **Felt:** "The Tolle gang no longer rides the hill road; Kell's riders have learned the drovers' path and watch where it meets the road."
- **Surface · scrutiny · machinery:** surface — a known trail drawn behind the toggle; scrutiny — the body's radius and its route choice's reason ("they keep to the forest paths: Kell hunts them"); machinery — the graph and its costs.

---

## §6 SUPPLIES, SIZE, COHESION AND THE SEASONS — the counterforce that stops runaways (BD-1; the owner's ~18:1x "require reinforcements and supplies", ~19:3x "supplies also constitutes fight readiness, size, and ability to be. sites help them ot maintain cohesion", "Beasts simply need bodies for food and have to procreate to reproduce so beasts should mostly be seasonal")

### 6.1 The supply stock
- **Reads:** TRUTH — the week's resolved acts (a raid's take, a waylaid stretch's attributed losses, a beast's kills, a flock's alms from its followers' share and piety).
- **Writes:** `bodySupply01` (one writer, `bodyLedger.js`). Upkeep each week `BODY_UPKEEP[bodyKind][might] × SEASON_NEED[season]` (DRAFT; larger bodies eat more; ONE seasonal need read for all three kinds — winter raises a gang's and a flock's need as it raises a pack's hunger, L-BD 38); gains by kind: a GANG from its takes (a raid's `succeeds`, its attributed share of the stretch's fired losses, smuggling through a strong-standing gate, a bribe's return, an employer's pay when `employed`); a CONCLAVE from ALMS — its followers' share × local piety where it stands or preaches (a flock among the faithful is fed; a flock on the road lives on what it carries), the ALMS every town in its radius grants it (§6.6) and an `endowed` court's gift — and, from the courts that curb or outlaw it (no alms there; BD14), its TAKES (its `raid`'s `succeeds`, §4.2); a BEAST from KILLS (§6.3). Every body that is not a beast may also be fed by a SPONSOR's seasonal flow (§10.4), a RANSOM paid (§9.10) or a forced SALE's price (§9.4); a subservient gang pays its GUILD's share (§6.8).
- **Its three meanings (BD9):** READINESS `bodyReadiness01 = clamp01(bodySupply01 / BODY_NEED[bodyKind][might])`, worded `provisioned · strained · starving` (the war's own supply words, `warResolve.js :: supplyBand`); SIZE (§6.2); EXISTENCE (§6.4).
- **Counterforce:** guarding the caravans, hunting the raids, keeping the villages' grain behind walls — every one lowers the take, so "guard the caravans and the gang starves" is arithmetic, not prose.

### 6.2 Size — recruitment and shedding (reinforcements)
- **Reads:** `bodyHabitat` at the body's country (truth for the recruitment pool; the habitat is the world's), its ties (an allied gang's merge, §9.1; a sister flock's `merged`; a beast's spring), the trails' capacity (§5.2).
- **Writes:** `might` one band up when a `recruit` week (or a conclave's `preach` week among the faithful) finds habitat over the floor AND readiness `provisioned` for DRAFT 4 consecutive weeks; one band down when `starving` for DRAFT 4 weeks (it sheds), when it loses a fight (the band contest's loser), or when it splits on a `scatter`. A gang that outgrows the trails (`capable`+) must take the road (a counterforce). Reinforcement by ties: a `merged` meeting folds the smaller into the larger (§9.1).
- **Clock:** monthly-ish dwell (DRAFT 4 weeks), never more than one band a month.

### 6.3 The beasts' year (the owner's ~19:3x word)
A beast's supplies are KILLS: livestock on the land (the country's herds — EC's resource read where lit, else the tier's agrarian share; never a second yield law, ROUTE EC), travellers on the roads, migrants, a patrol it outmatches. By SEASON (the landed season read; the climate band when it lands, the slot): SPRING — a `provisioned` pack at its lair BREEDS (`bred`, §3.2) and new packs are `wild_born` in the cover; SUMMER–AUTUMN — it hunts its territory; WINTER — hunger drives it to the ROADS (its presence term ×1.2 on the stretches, DRAFT; "winter brings the pack to the roads", the climate consumer the owner accepted) and its upkeep rises; when the kills stop it STARVES and ends `starved`. So the beast population BREATHES with the year — a seasonal presence, mostly, never a standing one; a fed pack breeds next spring, a starved one shrinks and dies (receipted). No recruitment, no ties (BD10). A cleared lair is an EMPTY den (ST7: empty = no effect) a gang may take; a beast-free region's hunting and forage yield is EC's resource read (ROUTE EC; cite, never a second yield law). A settled region — POP's growth raising towns' tiers — shrinks the wild's habitat (fewer births); a frontier breeds more; hunting packs to nothing costs riders (PR's losses) and wins only until the next spring's `wild_born`.

### 6.4 Cohesion and existence
- **Cohesion:** `bodyCohesion01` decays each week by `COHESION_DECAY.based` (DRAFT 0.01) with a held base, `COHESION_DECAY.roaming` (DRAFT 0.06) without; `rally` (conclave) and a won fight raise it; a lost fight, a `starving` week and a dead leader's fall (a named person, the DM's) lower it. A body with a LEADER (§6.10) holds its cohesion ON the leader: while the leader stands it decays at the based rate wherever it is (a captain holds a band together), and the leader's LOSS — killed by the DM, taken into custody (ES-2b's slot), turned by a court's hire (CH's clandestine idiom), or removed by the DM's `BODY_LEADER` — drops it at once by DRAFT 0.3 and tests FISSION (§6.7): a band without its captain scatters or splits. Below DRAFT 0.2 a body may only `move` (to a site) or `scatter`.
- **Existence:** a body ends from a fact (BD2): `starved` (supplies at zero for DRAFT 3 weeks — gang, beast), `scattered` (cohesion below 0.1, or a fight lost at `thin`, or its own `scatter` choice — gang; and a moving CONCLAVE whose supplies stand at zero for DRAFT 3 weeks — its people drift to the towns, §6.9's SCATTERED), `dissolved` (a conclave received into a town by `attemptEntry`, or one whose court turned `permitted` while it stood in that town — it is that town's row again), `merged` (absorbed in a meeting), `raised` (the DM's promotion, BD16), `disbanded` (the DM's end). Each end leaves its `ended` row (the ring) and a Herald line; its pairs drop; its site passes by ST's ONE writer in ST's own words — an ENDED body's site falls unheld, `cleared` (ST §6.1 step 1), a body that LEFT by its own `move` leaves it `abandoned`, and a body ended `raised` HANDS its site to the menace (the holder re-pointed to `{ kind: 'menace', id }`, never emptied, no unmaking telling, no reclaim); its HABIT rows drop through HB's actor-ended prune (§4.4); its standings stop composing (no write: §7.3 reads the live records).

### 6.5 Surface · scrutiny · machinery
Surface — only an act's line, and the map piece's condition word (`provisioned · strained · starving`) behind the toggle ("the Tolle gang, hungry"); scrutiny — the body's card: its size, supply word, cohesion word (DRAFT `holding · fraying · breaking`), its base and radius, the reasons of its last choice; machinery — the numbers.

---

### 6.6 THE ALMS — what a peaceable flock asks of the towns (the owner, ~19:4x: "conclaves that are not hostile actually request alms from all settlements how much they get is related to how distant their diety is from the settlement's patron and the prosperity of the settlement related to its tier and size")
- **Who asks, and where:** a conclave asks alms of EVERY settlement within its operating radius (§5.4) WHOSE COURT DOES NOT HOLD IT `curbed` OR `outlawed` — toward those courts it is hostile (BD14) and asks nothing there: it takes or starves — ONCE PER SEASON per town (DRAFT; the push budget), a STANDING card in that town's week, CLOCKED while the town has not answered (BD13): `alms_requested` (§12). The owner's "request alms from all settlements" is read as every settlement the flock can REACH — its operating radius (one week from its shrine, two when roaming the road) — because a request is carried on foot and its answer is a supply the flock must carry home, and an unbounded ask would put a card in every town each season against the push budget; a flock that wants a far town's alms moves toward it (`move`); vetoable — the alternative is every town of its creed's lands.
- **How much — ONE read, TWO factors:** `almsOf(worldState, snapshot, bodyId, settlementId) → { band, reasons }` = (i) the ALIGNMENT DISTANCE between the flock's god and the town's PATRON, read as the SAME `stanceOf(patron, the flock's god)` net that sets the welcome (§7.1 — one read, two consumers): near → generous, far → little, a town with no patron reads neutral; × (ii) the town's PROSPERITY RELATIVE TO ITS TIER AND SIZE through ST's ONE settlement band `settlementFundingBand` (its `prosperityRank01` — never a second ladder): `unfunded` gives nothing, `meagre` little, `funded` a share, `lavish` freely. The band is a SUPPLY gain on the flock (`bodySupply01`, DRAFT steps UNMEASURED) and the DEFAULT of the card.
- **The pool is WF-7's slot (measured, `cure/D-BD-r4.md` m1):** the alms would draw on the purse the patron temple draws on, but "⛔ `templeWealth` HAS NO WRITER IN THE ENGINE (a WF-7 design-doc future …)" — so until WF-7 (and W-COIN for coin) lands no coin leaves the town; the alms are the flock's supply gain and a fork input, and the town's own clergy feel it through the COUNTERFORCE below. ROUTE WF (WF-7: the temple purse the alms draw on, one writer).
- **The counterforce — the patron's own clergy:** alms given to a FAR god's flock press the town's court toward curbing it: each season's alms at `a share` or more enter the court's `body_stance` fork (§7.1) as ONE input, weighted by the PATRON creed's share and legitimacy in the town (a strong patron faith minds the silver going to a rival — item 21's gate); a near god's flock's alms move nothing.
- **The DM's three choices** on `alms_requested`: leave (the read's band is given), tilt (one band more or less), decide (`given` · `refused`); a refusal ends the clock and the flock leans to `move` (§4.2).
- **Felt:** "The Grey Lady's faithful asked alms of Ashford; the town gave freely." (scrutiny line — never pushed) · "Merewick gave the Red Friar's flock nothing; its own priests would not hear of it."

### 6.7 FISSION — the split that balances the merge (L-BD 35, the owner's 18:27 word)
JOIN existed (`merged`, §9.1) and SPLIT did not — a ratchet toward ever-larger bodies. One fission law per kind, each bounded by the cap per country (§3.4: a split that would exceed it does not happen — the larger simply sheds a band):
- **A gang SPLITS** when it is over its radius (its standings or its acts reach towns beyond `BODY_RADIUS_WEEKS` of its base — a grip it cannot hold) or when its cohesion falls below DRAFT 0.35 while it is `capable` or larger, or the week it loses its LEADER (§6.4): TWO records, the larger keeping the name, the base and the leader, the smaller born `spilled` from the parent (`birth.fromId` the parent's id; the receipt names the cause: "the Hollow Road gang has split; a lesser band holds the ford"), each at one band below the parent, supplies divided by size. A remnant below the naming band is NAMELESS (BD23).
- **A conclave never splits on its own.** A creed's row in a town SCHISMS only by the faith system's landed contest (`religionState.js :: resolvePatronContest` — "A SCHISM: a rival shares the patron's own niche"); a moving flock stays one flock (one creed, one people) and ends by entry, by scattering or by its creed's row's fate (§6.9). No BD schism law.
- **A pack SPLITS BY TERRITORY** when it outgrows its range: `strong` or larger and its radius holds no unhunted kill-country left for a season → two packs, the young one `bred` from the parent in the most-covered neighbouring country of the parent's range. A beast pack never merges with a stranger pack (`bodies_meet` between packs: `fought` or `passed`, BD10).
- **Clock · counterforce:** tested weekly in the births step (§2.7 step 3); the cap and the dwell bound it (a source that split bears no other until its dwell).

### 6.8 THE GANG'S END — one of four (BD25; the owner, 18:43 · 18:45 · 18:46 · 18:47)
Every gang carries ONE `endGoal` (`GANG_END_GOALS`, §2.6), set at birth by its habitat and — once named and led — by its leader's axes (a greedy captain `retire`s, a soldierly one founds a `free_company`, a patient one makes a `hamlet`, an ambitious one builds the `guild`; DRAFT weights UNMEASURED), the DM's to set (`BODY_GOAL`, §11). It is readable on scrutiny ("the Hollow Road gang means to found a hall of swords") and never pushed.
- **(a) `retire` → DISPERSE.** When its supplies' COIN (the gang's `bodySupply01` above its need — the sustenance stock's surplus) passes a SATISFACTION band (DRAFT `bodySupply01 ≥ 0.9` for 8 weeks, UNMEASURED), the gang ends `dispersed`: its people go back to the towns where, by its own reckoning, it is NOT KNOWN (the towns no decayed ring of its own deeds covers — (e)'s one read, never the towns' belief records; 42 (e)) — the receipt names where they went ("the Hollow Road gang has scattered, rich; its men are in Ashford and Low Ford now"). When POP's people ledger runs they rejoin those towns' population through POP's ledger (the spill-out birth in reverse; ROUTE POP); until then a receipt only, no census credit (macro over micro; the birth's own rule).
  - "the towns no decayed ring of its own deeds covers" is RN's ONE NAMED READ `deedRingAt(worldState, actorRef, settlementId, tick)` (RN §2.3: the ring test over an actor's OWN deeds — BELIEF, the actor's own; no heard test; RN's one ring rule `RING_BY_DECAY`), called with the gang's ref — the same read as (e).
- **(b) `free_company` → A MERCENARY INSTITUTION (the owner, 18:45: "establishing a merecenary institution where approprate not become an irregular").** The gang founds the catalog's MERCENARY institution of the town's tier — `"Free company hall"` (town) or `"Mercenary quarter"` (city · metropolis) (`data/institutionalCatalog.js`; measured, `cure/D-BD-r4.md` m6: NOT a new institution kind) — in a town where the tier admits it and NONE exists, in a DIFFERENT REGION (below); the gang ends `dissolved` (`into: 'institution'`); the institution counts as POWER in that town (the owner's definition) and SUPPLIES the LANDED market `worldPulse/mercenaryMarket.js` — its own town's shortfall, through the market's presence read (`mercPresenceOf(settlement)`: "how much hireable force a settlement's standing institutions represent"), `mercSupplementOf` untouched; a CROSS-COURT hire is CH's CORPS alone; an institution contracting ABROAD is WC's parked force market unparked — owner-gated, WC's. The founding goes through the ONE institution writer (`institutionLifecycle.js :: applyInstitutionLifecycleOutcome`, `fate: 'founded'`; the host is AT the 800 ceiling — BD's leaf calls the exported writer and adds NO host line; if a founding cause arm is needed a LEAF-FIRST car moves lines out first — BD-8's car-0, PLAUSIBLE). The champion-grade company — a CORPS paragon with a captain and fame — stays DM-AUTHORED custom content (the owner's 06:32 word); the engine never produces one. Being hired as IRREGULARS (`employed`, §9.8) stays a WARTIME TIE, never an end.
- **(c) `hamlet` → A SETTLEMENT FOUNDED IN PLACE (the owner, 18:47: "the hamlet is different, they have attained something like independence by holding the site for so long").** A gang that has HELD its base site long enough with funds enough (the hold's TENURE — the tick of the newest holder-change event in ST's site `events` ring, a `taken` or `placed` whose `by` is this body — older than DRAFT 156 weeks, and `bodySupply01 ≥ 0.75`, UNMEASURED; ST's record has a site-level `sinceTick` and an 8-row events ring but no holder-tenure field, so where the taking has been evicted from the ring the tenure reads AT LEAST the ring's oldest row — a lower bound that only understates a long hold; ST EXPORTS `holderSinceOf` for this read, and once ST carries it the tenure reads `holderSinceOf(worldState, siteId)` — the exact tick, the ring's lower bound retired then; until then the bound stands) founds a HAMLET over that site, IN PLACE: born UNSWORN (no liege; PL's fealty question may be asked of it later; it may be claimed, besieged or sworn like any settlement), carrying the PAIR STATES the gang earned (hostile with the towns it raided, tolerated where it was tolerated — the landed relationship labels written onto the new settlement's pairs by the founding writer, one writer), its first ruling power the gang's leadership as an INSTITUTION (no named fate; the captain is a founder, never a character the engine names), the site persisting under it (the owner's sites-3 word, ST14); its people are the gang's and whoever the site drew. ⛔ MEASURED (`cure/D-BD-r4.md` m7): master has NO runtime settlement-founding writer — so end (c) is a SLOT, DARK until POP's runtime founding lands (a new settlement record at runtime is the OWNER's capability: ⭐ OD-BD-35); until then a gang whose goal is `hamlet` holds its site and lives on (its goal unreached, readable on scrutiny). ROUTE POP.
- **(d) `guild` → THE GUILD, OR SUBSERVIENCE, OR THE GUILD WAR.** A gang may BUILD the criminal institution its town's TIER and SIZE admit — the catalog's own rules, measured (`data/institutionalCatalog.js`; `cure/D-BD-r5.md`): the THIEVES' GUILD where the `criminalPower` exclusive group holds NO member in that place ("`exclusiveGroup: 'criminalPower'`" — a city's `"Thieves' guild chapter"` at 10,000 souls or more, a metropolis's `"Thieves' guild (powerful)"`; the group's other member, `"Multiple criminal factions"` (city · metropolis), also fills the slot, so where it stands no guild is founded and the gang is one more faction in that turf, its standing composing as any gang's, §7.3); the `"Assassins' guild"` (metropolis only, NO exclusive group) where none already stands; the TOWN tier holds no criminal-power member, so a town-born gang whose goal is the guild seeks a city or a metropolis (the different-region rule, (e), moves it) — in a DIFFERENT REGION (below), through the same one institution writer; the gang ends `dissolved` (`into: 'institution'`) and the guild counts as power. Where a THIEVES' GUILD DOES exist, the gang is SUBSERVIENT to it (`subservientTo` the guild's town): a TIE — the guild takes a SHARE of every take (DRAFT one fifth, out of `bodySupply01`, UNMEASURED) and the gang is the guild's arm on the roads (its standing in that town composes with the guild's floor as any gang's does, §7.3) — UNLESS it tries the OVERTHROW: THE GUILD WAR (`contest: { kind: 'guild_war', townId, sinceTick }`), whose only interesting outcome the owner named ("it sparks a thieves guilds war which drastically increases crime and lowers internal security until one or both (the institution or the gang) are disposed of") — carried on the ONE channel both of the town's reads consume: while the war lasts the guild's town carries ONE ACTIVE CONDITION, `guild_war`, written through the landed condition writer `activeConditions.js :: withActiveCondition` (the kernel's own inline-condition idiom for `council_schism`) by BD's leaf, its archetype registered as ONE declared template row in `CONDITION_ARCHETYPE_TEMPLATES` at BD-8 (the one-row host edit, 597 eff), its `affectedSystems: ['defense_readiness', 'social_trust']` and its archetype a member of `pressureModel.js :: DEFENSE_ARCHETYPES` (ONE token, BD-8 — the SAME channel and weight class as the landed `war_pressure`) — so the ONE affectedSystems join (`causalState.js :: applyConditions`: "THE single active-condition scan") TAXES its defence readiness (`deriveDefenseReadiness`) and the defence pressure takes the archetype's "`defense += 0.16;`" once — the town's internal security falls through the ONE security read the kernel's `security01 = 1 − defence pressure` and BD's own `bodySecurity` read, NEVER a write of BD's and never a second security law (BD8). MEASURED (`cure/D-BD-r6-probe/guildwar-r6.out`, sixteen generated towns and cities on master's own pipeline): the security FALL is 0.189 at severity 0.5, 0.203 at 0.8 and 0.217 at 1.0 (median), against the landed `war_pressure`'s 0.231 at 0.5 — "drastically" delivered; where a DEFENSE archetype already stands (2 of 16) the archetype step is deduped and the fall is the readiness term alone (0.029), the law `war_pressure` itself obeys there (0.086). NO CRIME TERM: the guild war is ORDER's collapse, not crime's growth — the crime the owner names is the war ITSELF, two criminal powers fighting for the quarter (the gang's standing composing above the guild's floor in `criminalPowerOf` while both stand, §7.3) and what the lowered watch lets loose (§2.5's `bodySecurity` falls, so the quarter breeds), never a raised crime PRESSURE: measured, the crime pressure moves 0, so NO stressor birth is touched through crime (measured: a crime term of the drafted size moves it +0.08 · +0.12 · +0.15 and crosses `criminal_corridor`'s 0.56 and `infiltration`'s 0.58 from a 0.47 base). THE CONFLICT FACE, declared and bounded: the conflict pressure reads the same readiness inversely ("`let conflict = pressureFromScore(scores.defense_readiness);`"), so the war raises it by +0.086 · +0.143 · +0.171 (severity 0.5 · 0.8 · 1.0) — never the +0.18 of `CONFLICT_ARCHETYPES`, which `guild_war` does NOT join — and the worst measured base plus that rise (0.537) stays below every conflict-reading birth threshold (`monster_raider_pressure` 0.60 · `mass_migration` 0.62 · `wartime` 0.64 · `insurgency` 0.68 · `rebellion` 0.70 · `siege` 0.72 · `occupation` 0.76); a town whose OWN conflict already stands within 0.17 of a threshold (a town at war) may cross it at the war's peak, and that birth is the landed stressor's own, by its own gate — the condition's severity is the bound. ITS LIFE (§15.2): the template row declares `defaultStatus: 'stable'` (no invented drift — the landed `SEVERITY_DRIFT_PER_TICK` moves only `worsening` and `easing`) and `defaultExpiresAtTicks: GUILD_WAR_LEASE` (DRAFT 3 condition ticks, twelve weeks at the landed "`one_week:   0.25,`"); BD's leaf RE-ASSERTS the condition EACH WEEK the war stands (`withActiveCondition` replaces by id, `duration.elapsedTicks` 0) with WORLD provenance — `triggeredAt.sourceEventType: 'WORLD_PULSE_GUILD_WAR'`, `causes: [{ source: 'world_pulse', … }]` — so a DM's regenerate KEEPS it (`reconcile.js :: isWorldAuthoredCondition`: "`WORLD_CONDITION_SOURCE_PREFIXES = Object.freeze(['WORLD_PULSE', 'WORLD_STRESSOR', 'PARTY_ACTION', 'REGIONAL'])`"), the war's close LIFTS it (`withoutActiveCondition`), and with `bodyEffectsEnabled` dark mid-war nothing re-asserts it, so it EASES over its last weeks (`EXPIRY_EASING_WINDOW_TICKS` 2) and EXPIRES within the lease (`withExpiredConditionsRemoved`) — no ghost; its SEVERITY is the war's (DRAFT: `0.5` at the war's first week, rising one step a month to `0.8` while both stand, UNMEASURED); the commons' grievance rises as INT's commons voice reads the town's order (no write of BD's).
