## §9 ENGAGEMENTS — every one a hearing in its week (BD-4; the owner's ~18:3x "if a cult and a gang meet up, something, idk what is bound to happen unless they choose ot walk past each other", ~18:5x "extend these skirmishes to sites when held by settlement, beast, gang, or conclave", ~19:3x "every engagement, skirmish, terrorizing a town, is a hearing … they may terrorize thorpes, hamlets, and villages … in addition to immigrants, maybe sometimes patrols where they deem it appropriate, and caravans")

### 9.1 Two bodies meet — `bodies_meet` (NEW; BD owns)
- **The meeting:** two bodies in the same place in the same week (a country, a stretch, a trail, a site's attachment) meet through THE PATROLS' ONE encounter law WIDENED to body × body (PR §8.1 owns it — ROUTE PR): MET is co-location (truth); NOTICED is the law's keyed draw with each side's COVER (a body on a way reads that way's `cover01` from `hiddenWayEdges`; on the ROAD its size's visibility — `ROAD_COVER_BY_MIGHT` `thin` 0.5 · `modest` 0.3 · `capable` 0.15 · `strong`/`formidable` 0, DRAFT, UNMEASURED: a handful can pass for travellers, a war band cannot — and +0.2 in a country whose commons SUPPORT it, §7.6) and its SEEK (a gang's `feud` choice, a flock's `confront` against the gang that terrorizes its people, a `hunt` stance toward a beast, a hungry pack weight it up; a gang carrying loot weights it down). Unnoticed, they pass and nothing is minted.
- **Verdicts:** `passed · parleyed · fought · merged`. **DEFAULT** by the pair's label (§7.2) and a keyed draw weighted by each body's current act (stream `bodies_meet:<pairKey>:<tick>`): `hostile` → `fought` (the weaker may slip away: `passed` at the band contest's odds); `rival` → `passed` or `fought`; `neutral` → `passed` or `parleyed`; `allied` → `parleyed` or `merged`. `merged` joins the smaller into the larger — gang with gang, flock with flock of ONE creed — never a conclave with a gang (it may SHELTER it: `parleyed` with a shelter incident, a tie). A beast met: `fought` where it is hungry and outmatches, else `passed` (it avoids).
- **`fought` resolves on THE HOUSE'S BAND CONTEST** — `bodyStrength` against `bodyStrength` on `SIEGE_VERDICT_BANDS`, one keyed draw (ST's `site_cleared` and PR's `patrol_engages` use the same contest) — the loser falls a band (scatters at `thin`) and loses cohesion; the winner takes DRAFT a third of the loser's supplies. The brief's "the landed field-battle read" (`armyTransit.js :: resolveFieldBattle`) takes ARMY records (`{ armyId, size, readiness, supplyQuality, … }`); the house's band-scale contest is the siege band read ST and PR already use for dens, sites and detachments, so a body fight is that contest — one law for band-scale forces, none minted here.
- **An ARMY met:** the same kind, the army's settlement as the counterparty (the army ref on the `subject` dial): a gang `passed` (it never fights a column — it scatters to the trail; caught in the open it ends `scattered`); beasts `fought` (hunted at the army's size) or `passed`; a conclave `passed` (protected) or `fought` (SACKED) by the army's court's alignment (`stanceOf(its patron, the flock's god)`) — §9.8.
- **Class CLIMAX; WHERE** `country (a)` for a meeting in a country, `road (a, b)` for a meeting on a stretch or on the trail that runs beside it (the card names it "in the forest beside the hill road"), `site` at a site. The card is NEVER PUSHED unless the DM pinned the country or the party stands near (BD21). A meeting is one event, CLIMAX by the registry's own definition (never item 9's "STANDING unless pinned, then CLIMAX"); "behind the scenes" is the push budget's work, not a class.

### 9.2 A body terrorizes a settlement — the registry's `raid`, REUSED (one kind, one vocabulary)
- **The kind exists:** the fold §3.4's `raid` ("the war volume (border incidents) | the raid's landing | `repelled · succeeds · bought_off` | CLIMAX"), the landed incident type `incidentType: "raid"` (`relationshipRulesAdversarial.js`) its settlement-to-settlement producer. THE BODIES WIDEN ITS RAIDERS to a body (ROUTE FOLD and the war volume; one kind, never a second). The brief's new kind `raid` with `repelled · raided · sacked` ("measured against MN's and WC's raid words first") is not minted: measured, the registry already holds the kind and its words; `sacked` is never an engine verdict (BD15).
- **Producer — THE HEARING'S PHYSICS, TRUTH (BD12's split: the CHOICE weighed the visible walls, the PRODUCER reads the threshold):** a gang's `raid`, a HOSTILE flock's `raid` (§4.2) or a beast's `prowl` choice on a settlement inside its radius WHERE THE DEFENCE THRESHOLD HOLDS (BD14): `bodyStrength(body) > bodyDefenceOf(T, bodyKind) × (1 + RAID_MARGIN)` (DRAFT 0.2), `bodyDefenceOf` = `defenseLedger(T)`'s `military` (gang, flock) or `monster` (beast), the walls' and the garrison's terms removed while their institutions stand `ruined`, plus THE SITES' at-hand keep term (`siteReadinessOf`'s readiness × `BODY_KEEP_POINTS`, BD14) — never `homeDefense` and never a tier rule; a hostile flock's `raid` lands only upon the land of a court that curbs or outlaws it (BD14 — per court). Where it fails the act turns to the settlement's traffic (§9.6) or a softer target in the radius; no card for a raid that cannot be.
- **The card:** CLIMAX in the target's week; WHERE `settlement`; `{ kind: 'body', bodyKind, id }` (on the `subject` dial) upon `{ kind: 'settlement', id }` (the anchor). DEFAULT: the band contest of the body's strength against `bodyDefenceOf` (one keyed draw, stream `raid:<bodyId>:<targetId>:<tick>`): `succeeds` — the take (supplies; for a gang, recruits — the village's young who go with it, a band step at most; a beast, livestock) and a BOUNDED loss on the target through the landed sinks (its food through EC's stock where lit, else nothing; deaths through the LIVE population writer — ONE population outcome `{ id: 'body.raid.<bodyId>.<targetId>.<tick>', type: 'population', candidateType: 'body_raid', headline, populationDeltas: [{ saveId: <targetId>, delta: −deaths, reason }] }` applied by `populationDynamics.js :: applyPopulationOutcomeToSettlement` on the stage's `settlementUpdates` channel (the calamity host's own `applyExodusToUpdates` idiom; `fieldManifest.js`'s population `pulseWriter`; its `populationHistory` breadcrumb the receipt), capped DRAFT one hundredth of the village — never `peopleLedger.js`'s `mortality` sink, "DARK-COMPLETE BY ABSENCE OF CALLERS" (measured, `cure/D-BD-p5.md` m6–m7)); `repelled` — the body loses cohesion and supplies ("they scattered and went on, hungrier" — WC's REPEL in WC's sense); `bought_off` — the DEMAND answered: the town's fork offers it where its defence is below the body and its prosperity allows (the landed treasury band); the body's standing rises there and its `raid` weight at that town rises (a habit — WC §1.8.1's warning "BUY-OFF … TEACHES RAIDING PAYS"; ROUTE WC for the habit system's row). A conclave never raids a court that permits or endows it (there its prey is converts and alms, through `preach`); toward a court that curbs or outlaws it, it may — for supplies and bodies, weighted by its god's axes (the owner's 19:3x word lists conclaves among the bodies that terrorize the rural band "for supplies and bodies"). CAPTIVES: the `succeeds` take's "bodies" are UNNAMED — a COUNT on the body's size (recruits, or labour on its supplies: a band step at most), never people the engine tracks; a raid's default never takes a NAMED person (custody waits on ES-2b, BD27) — the DM may decide a taking on the card, which opens THE RANSOM RACE when ES-2b lands (§9.10) (OPENED, by delegation: live design, built after ES's custody wave); a HOSTILE flock may take one where its god's axes allow it — an evil or warlike god's flock readily, a good one rarely and only in extremity (starving, hunted), never "never" (the owner, 18:54: "if they are taken by a gang or conclave").
- **The DM's three choices:** leave, tilt (a draw bias ±0.05 / ±0.15), decide (`repelled · succeeds · bought_off`). A burned village is the DM's act through the landed roads, never a raid verdict.

### 9.3 A body and the patrols — PR's `patrol_engages`, REUSED; THE HUNT (the owner's ~19:0x word)
- **The fight:** a body MET by riders (PR §8.1, truth) whom their court marks it `hunted`/`outlawed` (and every beast), or a body that seeks out a patrol it outmatches (BD14's "patrols where they deem it appropriate"), is PR's `patrol_engages` — `driven_off · screened · withdrew · cut_down`, PR's band contest, PR's card (PR §5.3's `patrol_engages` row reads THE BODIES' body row and §5.5 THE HUNT; BD supplies `bodyStrength` and the cover term — the way's `cover01`, the road's `ROAD_COVER_BY_MIGHT`, §9.1). One fact, one hearing.
- **THE HUNT (ROUTE PR — the hunt is PR's text; BD cites):** PR §5.5 carries it (`predictedPlaceOf` · `trailWatchOf` · `pursuedRef` · the cold trail, the unridden round, shadow-and-group, over-pursuit); `BODY_HABITS` is the KIND's prior, read only where HB carries no rows for THIS body (§4.4); a NAMED body only is predicted by name (a nameless one has no NAMED record, BD23 — the riders ride against the brigandage the town has heard of and the SIGHTINGS its court holds of it, BD23's one exception). A HOSTILE body plans on believed coverage and prefers unknown trails (§5.3); a patrol OFF its routine round PREDICTS and PURSUES — PR's `pursue` aim WIDENED from the party's watch to bodies: the prediction is the patrol's town's BELIEF of the body's position (`believedBodyOf`, §2.4 — its last told act and its court's SIGHTINGS, the trails the town KNOWS, the kind's HABITS — a gang returns to its den, a pack follows the season, a flock goes where the faith is fractured; BD supplies the closed `BODY_HABITS` read); the catch is PR's encounter law (truth); the trail goes COLD after DRAFT 3 weeks without a sighting and the round resumes; a hunting patrol's round GOES UNRIDDEN (presence drops elsewhere — the gang's chance; one counterforce); a patrol OUTMATCHED SHADOWS and calls for grouping (PR's group/disperse; never engages — the second counterforce); over-pursuit as PR names it. A `permitted` conclave is NEVER pursued; beasts always.
- **Defeat moves standings (BD8):** a body `driven_off` or `cut_down` loses its standing in that court's land (§7.3) and the court's legitimacy and the watch's reputation rise by the landed reads (§7.3's delta row).

### 9.4 Skirmishes at sites — ST's kinds, actor lists WIDENED (ROUTE ST; the fold §3.4 WHERE `site`)
- **A body arriving at an UNHELD site** → ST's `site_taken` (`held_by_them · driven_off`) with the body as the arriver; an AT-HAND court that sees it sorties (ST's own default). Every arrival on a site — unheld, held by a body, held by a settlement or a faith — is REGISTERED through `arriveAt` and COMMITTED by BD's writer on `arrived` only (§2.7 step 5).
- **A body moving on a site another BODY holds** (a gang on a beast's lair, a good flock's militants on a gang's den, a gang on a rival's den) → ST's `site_cleared` (`cleared · repelled`), the clearer the body, the holder the other body; a TIED court clearing a body's den → `site_cleared` under ST's ONE CONTEST PER SITE PER WEEK, ONE SIDE AT A TIME (ST §6.1 step 6d): the anchor court and the bodies it `harboured`s or `employed`s are one side, every other body its own challenger; when two challenger sides arrive in one week the stronger contests and the other HOLDS at the approach where it stood (the hold helper's FREEZE); after a won clearing the CLEARER NAMED holds the site.
- **A body attacking a site a SETTLEMENT or a FAITH holds** (a gang on a shrine, a pack on a watchtower) → ST's `site_siege` (`holds · falls · relieved · lifted`, STANDING), the attacker the body.
- **The defender** is the site's ONE readiness value × its kind's ceiling (`siteReadiness01 × SITE_DEFENSE_CEILING[kind]`; ST's words `at_strength · manned · sparse · skeleton · hollow`) PLUS the holder body's `bodyStrength` where a body holds it; the attacker its `bodyStrength`; ST's contest (the band contest), ST's verdict words. Holders of all four kinds (`settlement · faith · person · untied`) sit on both sides. A held site is the body's base and cohesion (BD9); its abandonment is ST's `abandoned` through ST's writer (a body holder is EXEMPT from ST's abandonment by want — moving the base is a choice, never a drift, BD9; ST's R5). A MENACE arriving at a body's site is ST's `site_cleared` with the MENACE the clearer (the holder body the defender). A body PROMOTED by the DM (`raised`, BD16) HANDS its den to the menace it became: ST's one writer re-points the holder to `{ kind: 'menace', id }`, the site is NOT cleared, no unmaking telling is emitted and no reclaim is offered (MN's reading).
- **A PEACEABLE FLOCK AND A SITE (the owner, ~19:4x: "non hostile conclaves can occupy a site or attack one hosted by hostile forces. In such cases, they may not be pursued by settlement but may be forced to give it up in exchange for resources"):** a flock no court in its reach holds `outlawed` may TAKE an EMPTY site (ST's `site_taken`, the flock the arriver; it holds as `faith`, §2.1) or CLEAR a site a HOSTILE body holds (ST's `site_cleared`, the flock the clearer — a good god's militants on a gang's den, §4.2), and the town does NOT pursue it for either (pursuit is by stance, §7.1; PR §5.5). "Forced to give it up in exchange for resources" is ST's OWN kind `site_sale` (ST §6.1 step 6b, §8; ST's own kind — CLIMAX, WHERE `site`, verdicts `sold · refused`): an attached court that holds the flock's creed `permitted` or `curbed` DEMANDS the site against a price; the FLOCK answers by its god's recorded axes (ST's `saleFork`, no randomness: lawful yields at a fair price, chaotic may refuse, good yields where the people are served); `sold` pays the price INTO the flock's supplies through BD's one writer (`bodySupply01` + DRAFT 0.5, UNMEASURED; the court's coin is the W-COIN slot) and the flock leaves the site (ST writes the event `sold` through its one writer); `refused` moves the court's `body_stance` fork ONE STEP toward `curbed` (§7.1 — the counterforce; the court's next act is its own hearing). A SEIZURE instead of a sale is ST §6.1 step 6c, open only under `outlawed`, or `curbed` after a refused `site_sale` — never by a stance flip in the same act (§7.1). No new verb.
- **THE REPAIR CEILING (the owner, ~19:4x: "there is a cieling to how much conclaves and gangs can bolster or repair it for they lack the logistics. Beasts never repair it."):** a gang- or conclave-held FORTIFICATION is kept on ST's own read with the BODY's SUPPLIES as its funding — ST's `BODY_FUNDING_BY_SUPPLY` maps `bodySupply01` onto ST's `FUNDING_BANDS` NEVER above `meagre` (no logistics), so its `siteReadiness01` reaches at most `manned` (ST measured: a provisioned gang's sound keep 0.45, `manned`; a flock's at most `sparse`, × ½ as a faith holder of a fortification), and its REPAIR reaches at most `worn` (`BODY_REPAIR_CEILING`; ST measured `SITE_QUALITY = sound · worn · ruined`, so the brief's "ceiling `sound`" would bound nothing — `sound` is the top). BEASTS NEVER REPAIR: a lair's quality only DECAYS by ST's wear law (an untied site wears a band every second year). THE PATRON'S LIFT: a flock `endowed` by a court (§7.1; the word kept for exactly this) repairs with that court's logistics — its ceiling lifts to the PATRON court's own effective band (ST6's band through the patron) — the one concrete thing patronage buys a flock, and the reason a court endows the flock that holds the keep on its border.

### 9.5 A body and a named person — CH's, PT's and the roads' own kinds, never a new one of BD's; SCRUTINY AND RETALIATION on public missions (the owner, 18:52)
A body meeting a PARAGON on its leg is CH's road arm (CH's kind; the body the counterparty; ROUTE CH). A body meeting the PARTY is THE PARTY's kind on its road arm, its default "nothing has happened to the party yet" (requirement 24; ROUTE PT); it happens ONLY when the DM's own move of the party crosses a stretch a body works — BD never moves a body toward the party and no choice reads a party fact (BD15).
- **AN ENVOY ON A BANDIT ROAD — the landed gauntlet, ATTRIBUTED** (measured, `cure/D-BD-r4.md` m3: PR §8.2's "never harms" is the RIDERS' law; on master the roads gauntlet already harms envoys on bandit roads): the frozen `roadsKernel.js` PASS 3 — "In transit, a bandit road (T3) can still take an embassy that no army meets (bandits do not parley)" — rolls `hostage` or `robbed` for a roads mission on a hop whose embattlement reaches `ROADS_TUNING.EMBATTLED_THRESHOLD`. That is THE ONE interception law for a body's hand on an envoy: a body's row raises the hop's level (§8.2), so the SAME roll fires more where it rides, and a FIRED T3 outcome is ATTRIBUTED after the fact to the body whose row is largest on that hop (`bodyOnRoute` over the captor's country that tick, the `banditryLoss` idiom; the outcome READ from the landed records the pass already writes — CONFIRMED at master: a `hostage` writes the landed ransom record ("`captorId: res.captorId,`" · "`threatClass: res.cls,`" · "`startedTick: now2,`") and a `robbed` its news beat once ("`if (!m.robbedReceipted) { m.robbedReceipted = true; …`"), so no kernel line is needed) — "the Tolle gang robbed Kell's envoy on the hill road". The kernel is never edited (838, frozen); a `hostage` stays the LANDED roads hold (`RansomRec`, its captor the hop's town, its ransom the landed captor-and-home economy) — BD names the hand and adds the RETALIATION below; BD's OWN acts never take a named person into custody (BD27; ES-2b).
- **VISIBILITY AND VALUE (the owner: "hostile gangs and conclaves have greater risk scrutiny on caravans and NPCs that are contracted or publcly on an envoy mission"):** a caravan under PUBLIC CONTRACT (a WY-6 caravan body under a posted escort — a call-board escort entry, CH §14 / ST item 20's board; the flows of §9.6 are aggregate and carry no contract) and an ENVOY PUBLICLY ON MISSION (the errand spine's public errands — IN makes a public mission KNOWN on the road, so a body's BELIEF of the traveller is higher) are MORE VALUABLE and MORE VISIBLE to a HOSTILE body: its choice weights them up as prey (§4.1 (iv)) — a body seeks the stretch they ride (its row stands there, so the landed rolls fire there).
- **RETALIATION (the owner: "Else to have greater retaliation against them"):** BECAUSE they are public, striking them COSTS MORE. An envoy STOPPED or ROBBED by a body (an attributed T3 outcome; never CAUGHT by BD's own act) is an OUTRAGE — and it reaches the envoy's court AS A TELLING, NEVER AS THE ATTRIBUTION'S TRUTH: BD's word step (§2.7 step 8) writes the attributed outcome's rumor entry with the typed `bodyId` slot (§2.4) at the hop's WITNESS settlements and the rumor road carries it on (`hopDelayTicks`; the robbed envoy, home again, among its carriers); from the week the court HAS HEARD it, its forks read `believedBodyOf(worldState, courtId, bodyId)` — whom it believes struck: the envoy's court gains a GRIEVANCE and a CASUS on the LANDED grievance ladder against whoever it believes HARBOURS the body (a court's `harboured` stance is a public fact) or SPONSORS it (once the tie has LEAKED, §10.4) — §10.2 (a)'s `peace_unkept` patch AIMED at that harbourer or sponsor, and GR's demand road; never a second ladder — and its stance fork turns the body `hunted` with its BOUNTY fork opened (§9.9, §9.11); the ATTRIBUTION itself (`bodyOnRoute`) stays TRUTH for the receipt and the DM's scrutiny (who in fact). A NAMELESS body carries no `bodyId` slot (BD23): its outrage reaches the court as the brigandage it feeds — no named fork and no bounty. A contracted caravan robbed brings the CONTRACTOR's bounty and hunt the same way. A PRIVATE traveller (an uncontracted merchant, a SECRET envoy) draws less scrutiny and NO retaliation. The body's CHOICE = value × visibility AGAINST retaliation risk, weighted by its personality (a reckless captain stops the envoy; a careful one lets it pass and takes the private caravan behind) and its LESSONS (§4.4).
- **A WARDEN IN THE WAY (L-BD 41 (b)):** a town whose public-security office is held by a warden is more dangerous to a body (it knows the ways, §2.3), so a NAMED gang's own week may choose to BRIBE him (where his wages go unpaid — THE PATROLS' item-16 slot, PR) or to strike at him — the attempt is CH's `person_at_the_gate` ROAD-ARM card (CH §11's attempt arm: verdicts `withstood · bribed`, default `withstood`; WHERE `road (a, b)` with the way named, or `settlement`; `bribed` offered only where his wages go unpaid, its effect BD's own `bribe` row — the court's stance one step toward `tolerated` at its next `body_stance` fork), its ONE deed CH's `warden_attempted` (doer the named gang, target the warden; CH §2.2b's two rows); "or to KILL him" stays the gang's CHOICE, never an outcome — no verdict kills, and the warden's death is the DM's own `kill` (no named fate).

### 9.6 The traffic — caravans and migrants as FLOWS; one STANDING kind `traffic_waylaid` (NEW; BD owns)
- **Through the one road loss law:** a body's row raises its stretches' level (§8.2), so the SAME seeded roll (`banditryLoss` at the two arrival sites) fires more where it works; a FIRED roll is ATTRIBUTED to the body whose row is largest on the route (`bodyOnRoute`) — the receipt names the hand ("the Tolle gang took a tenth of Kell's grain on the hill road") and the body's take is its share of the shortfall (its supplies rise). The one host edit: each arrival site pushes `{ key, path, loss }` to an OPTIONAL sink when `bandit.fired` (absent ⇒ byte-identical; ROUTE EC/TR for the two lines in `commodityFlow.js` 474 and `supplyShipments.js` 210 — PLAUSIBLE until BD-2's car-0 measures that both arrival paths take the sink without a signature change). Migrants on the road die by `roadDeathRate`'s same level (deterministic at dispatch, conserved); a gang PREYING on migrants takes recruits from them (a band step at most) or, where the group is cast out, the group detaches its own gang (§3.2 (e)).
- **`traffic_waylaid` (STANDING; WHERE `road (a, b)`):** ONE card per (body, stretch) every week a body works a stretch's traffic (a gang's `raid` aimed at the traffic, a beast's `prowl` on a road); verdicts `waylaid · let_pass`; DEFAULT `waylaid` — the body's row stands on that stretch in the coming week and the landed rolls fire as they will; the DM's `let_pass` lifts the body's row from that stretch for the coming week (its presence and its take) — the DM's whole hand over a road; NEVER PUSHED (BD21). The brief's two STANDING kinds `caravan_waylaid` · `migrants_waylaid` are ONE fact (the body works the stretch; caravans and migrants are its traffic), and the flows' own losses fire inside their arrival and dispatch arithmetic, which a hearing cannot hold without re-plumbing two landed movers; so the card decides the body's COMING week, which requirement 24's pause can honour. A caravan that is a THING on the road is WY-6's kind (§9.7).

### 9.7 A caravan that is a thing — WY-6's `caravan_seized`, REUSED
Where WY-6's caravan bodies are lit (`caravanBodiesEnabled`), a body meeting a caravan on the road is WY-6's `caravan_seized` (`seized · ransomed · passed`, CLIMAX, WHERE `road`) with the BODY as the seizer — `ransomed` is the owner's "make demands of caravans" (the caravan pays to pass) — ROUTE WY (the seizer widened; one kind). Dark, the caravan is a flow (§9.6).

### 9.8 Armies in wartime (the owner's ~18:5x "if a military unit during wartime interacts with them... well, you can guess it")
- **(a) Gangs:** a gang never fights a column (§9.1: it scatters to the trail); WARTIME IS THE GANGS' SEASON — the levy takes the riders (PR's fielded count falls, so security falls and habitat rises), routs feed births (`deserted`, §3.2 (d)); a gang on an army's SUPPLY road feeds the supply shipments' banditry through its row (`supplyShipments.js`' arrival roll already reads the route's level — the landed interdiction; the naval `navalLayer.js :: blockadeInterceptsSupply` is a navy's, never a gang's) — one term; where the war volume's `supply_raid` (`repelled · succeeds`) is built, a gang's deliberate strike on an army's supply line is that kind with the body as raider (ROUTE war volume / WC); a wartime court EMPLOYS gangs as IRREGULARS against its enemy's supply (`employed`, §7.1's default proposal; CH's covert-hire idiom; a TIE, never a hire into the manpower pool).
- **(b) Beasts:** an army meeting a pack hunts it at its size (`bodies_meet` `fought`) or loses stragglers — a bounded attrition term on the march through the war machinery's ONE attrition read (ROUTE WC; BD supplies the beasts' presence on the army's path).
- **(c) Conclaves:** an army meeting a flock acts by its court's alignment — protected (`passed`) or SACKED (`fought`: the flock scattered; a DEED on RN's ledger, doer the army's settlement, travelling as a TALE (RN1); a sacred claim where WF's `sacredClaim.js` applies — ROUTE RN, WF).
- **(d) A menace and a gang:** a gang may JOIN a menace that recruits — only by the menace's OWN authored course (MN6: the course chooses; "a DM-authored course MAY name recruitment of bodies as one of its aims (the owner's menaces never emergent; recruitment is the course's authored act)"); the course word is MN's `enlists` (MN §6: the menace, arrived, holds X's country as `stays` does and is ENLISTING there — `menaceEnlisting(worldState, settlementId)`); ANY GANG whose `where` touches that country may come, NAMELESS included ("an authored course absorbs any gang whose where touches the country, nameless included (it merges; the menace's line names what matters)") — never a conclave (its wants are its creed's) and never a beast (a driven pack IS a menace, BD10); whether it comes is the GANG's own weekly choice under its keyed fork (§4: the menace's strength and the gang's hunger and hunt its weights); the gang that comes ends `merged`, BD's writer REQUESTING the absorption through MN's ONE writer `menaceLedger.js :: absorbBody(worldState, menaceId, { bodyId, might, tick })` (its callers a closed, walker-pinned list of one — THE BODIES' stage), which raises the menace's might ONE band at most where the gang is no more than one band below it, never above `formidable` and never while held (MN's rule, M58); the absorption is REFUSED `menace_not_enlisting` unless the menace stands `raiding` in the course's country, and a counted deserter gang's people leave by the people ledger's `dm_removed` sink with cause `menace_absorbed` (`peopleLedger.js :: CONSERVATION_ENDPOINTS`: "`SINKS: Object.freeze(['dm_removed', 'fell', 'mortality'])`"; MN §6) — the one road into a menace besides the DM's `PROMOTE_BODY` (BD16).
- **(e) The bought gate (L-BD 45):** a gang INSIDE a besieged town (based in its quarter, or holding a site at its walls) may be BOUGHT by the BESIEGER to open a gate — the clandestine hire on CH's ONE idiom (§7.1's covert hire, ES-6a's leak), a DEED on RN's ledger, its effect ONE bounded term on the siege verdict's defender read (WC's; the siege program's own seam — ROUTE WC so the siege program is not surprised; never a second siege law).
- **(f) Cold war comes half into the open (L-BD 46b):** a SPONSORED gang on the enemy's SUPPLY roads is (a)'s interdiction feeder with the sponsor's purpose as its weight; a SPONSORED flock against a besieged town's RESOLVE leans the war program's home-front costs (`warHomeCosts.js :: applyHomeWarCosts`, cited; one bounded term — ROUTE WC); irregulars stay (a)'s `employed` tie (§10.5).

### 9.9 THE BOUNTY AND THE CALL BOARD — the one hook by which the table's heroes and villains meet a body (BD23; L-BD 33 · 33b, the owner's 18:27 and 18:58 words)
- **Who may post, on whom:** a court may post a BOUNTY only on a NAMED body (BD23: "that is also when they get bounties") its stance marks `hunted` or `outlawed`, or on a beast pack the DM has named a menace (then MN's) — never on a `permitted`/`endowed` flock or a `tolerated`/`employed`/`harboured` gang (one stance, one act). The court's own fork proposes it (draw-free): `bodyThreat01` (§2.5) at or above `BOUNTY_THREAT_FLOOR` (DRAFT 0.5), the court's `settlementFundingBand` at `funded` or better, and no bounty of its own standing on that body; an OUTRAGE (§9.5) opens it the week the court has HEARD it.
- **The hearing:** `body_bounty` (§12) — CLIMAX in the court's week, WHERE `settlement`; verdicts `posted · withheld`; DEFAULT the fork's; the DM's three choices. `posted` writes the court's stance row's `bounty: { band, sinceTick }` (`courtStances`, §2.2; the purse BAND on the court's `FUNDING_BANDS`, never a coin figure).
- **The cost — the counterforce:** the purse is coin the treasury moves only when W-COIN lights (`treasury.js :: UPKEEP_LEDGERS` gains a `bounties` row then — the W-COIN slot, ROUTE the treasury's owner); until then the posting COSTS the court one step of its effective funding band for the season it stands (DRAFT), so a poor court cannot post forever and a rich one can — an INPUT ROW of ST's ONE band read `settlementFundingBand` ("a standing bounty lowers the court's band one step"; ROUTE ST — the band takes the input under `bodyEffectsEnabled`, BD never writes it), lit with `bodyEffectsEnabled` ONLY (it moves PR's pay gate and CH's retainers downstream, so the record alone stays byte-identical).
- **The call board:** a posted bounty is a CALL on CH's ONE call board (`paragon/callBoard.js :: callBoard`, a READ — CH §14; ST item 20's board), read from the ONE posted-bounty record, `courtStances[courtId][subjectKey].bounty` (`{ band, sinceTick }`, §2.2 — CH's board reads it and stores nothing), the call's `source` naming its body (`{ kind: 'body_bounty', courtId, bodyId }`, so PT's request takes `need: { bodyId }`), lit with `bodyEffectsEnabled`, in the `order` domain for a gang or a flock — an outlawed flock's bounty is an `order` call, never `faith` — and the `wildland` domain for a beast, at the court's town, its band the purse's; PARAGONS and CORPS answer it by CH's weekly choice (CH §4.4's `calling` weight), and THE PARTY by the DM's hand — PT's `party_hunted` MIRRORED: the party hunting a body is the party's own deed door, never a world act toward the party (ROUTE PT · CH: the board row; the party's half).
- **The claim:** a claimed bounty is the SAME deed as the hunt's success — CH's `hunt_won` row ("BD's bounty CLAIM is the same `hunt_won` row (no third deed)"; doer the claimer — the leader, the paragon or the company — target the body; THE PARTY's claim is PT's ONE deed `body_ended` (PT §3.1, OD-PT-33 — one deed: no `hunt_won` beside it); its defeat the outcome `snuffed_out` or `driven_off`, §9.11), paid from the purse to the claimer's court or company (W-COIN) — fame to the claimer (RN), and the bounty row DROPS. A bounty unclaimed for DRAFT 52 weeks lapses (requirement 23's horizon); a body that falls below the naming band keeps its bounty only while it keeps its name (BD23's dwell).
- **Felt:** "Kell has put a price on the Hollow Road gang." (surface, at posting) · "A free company out of Ashford took Kell's price on the Hollow Road gang." (surface, at the claim).

### 9.10 THE RANSOM RACE — the body's side of the custody slot (BD27; L-BD 34 · 34b · 25b, the owner's 18:54 and 18:55 words) (measured, `cure/D-BD-r4.md` m2)
⛔ DARK UNTIL ES-2b LANDS: a NAMED person taken by a body is CUSTODY, which waits on ES-2b (the owner's persisted-shape slot; the fold §3.3.13). This section writes the body's side so the slot has its shape. OPENED, by delegation ("keep them dark" reverses it): ES-2b's custody shape — and WR-7d's ransom persistence — are LIVE DESIGN dispatching in their own waves; the wave order stands: ES's custody wave before BD-4's ransom race — this race builds at BD-4 after ES's custody wave has landed (the build order, never a second gate) — and that wave INCLUDES the normalizer step BD27 names (a body-held captive kept under its `captiveId`, flag-blind for the declared shape), without which master's normalizer deletes the race's custody row on the next envoy-hold write and erases a second errand-less captive (`cure/D-BD-p5b-p5-probe/custody.out`).
- **Who:** a gang or a HOSTILE conclave — a flock where its god's axes allow it, as a WEIGHT, never an absolute (an evil or warlike god's flock readily; a good one rarely and only in extremity — starving, hunted; a peaceable flock never takes) — that takes a NAMED person (an envoy, a paragon; NEVER the party, whose fate is the table's) by the DM's decision on a card or by CH's road arm where ES-2b lets it hold. ≤ 2 named captives per body (`held`, §2.2).
- **The clock:** a RANSOM CLOCK starts at capture, `clockWeeks` = `max(4, 8 + a × fame01 + b × hopWeeks)` (DRAFT: `a` 8, `b` 1; UNMEASURED) — a FLOOR plus additive terms: the captive's standing (RN's fame of the person at the captor's base — `fameIn`'s scope, and its office, `ACT_OFFICE`, banded 0..1) and the road from the body's base to the captive's court (`hopWeeks`).
- **The two sides speak the LANDED vocabularies (`ransomChoices.js`):** the BODY is the CAPTOR (`CAPTOR_CHOICES = hold · ransom · release`: it HOLDS and DEMANDS — `ransom` — while it can feed him; it RELEASES when it cannot); the COURT is the HOME, and its weekly line on the registry's `ransom` row is the landed `HOME_ANSWERS = pay · refuse · abandon` (the fold §3.3.13: "The row takes the LANDED `pay · refuse · abandon`"), read through the landed home leaf (`ransomChoices.js :: homeRansomAnswer`'s bands — the captor's side is `captorRansomChoice`, the body's supplies standing in for the captor's books: lawfulness, morality, security, the court's means, its regard of the captive through RN's court regard — the `regardBand` projection, RULINGS-AFTER-RESEARCH §1). The court's CHOICES during the clock are `pay early · hunt · wait` (the brief's `pay · hunt · wait`; CH §12.4's words stand as the choices): `hunt` is the court's OTHER act (its stance `hunted`, its bounty, `hunt_taken` — §9.9, §9.11) and `wait` is requirement 24's LEAVE; the row's engine line is the landed `pay · refuse · abandon` read through `homeRansomAnswer` — one vocabulary on the row, the landed one; the two are one card: the DM's three choices and the court's own default line.
- **The race:** the BODY must SURVIVE the clock — evade the hunt (§9.3, §9.11), keep its supplies (a hostage COSTS: the captive's upkeep on `bodySupply01`, DRAFT), and hold its base — while the court answers week by week. The race's CLOSINGS, THREE WORDS FOR THREE EVENTS (the owner's 18:54 word: "if the gang can survive with teh NPC for long, then they will have the ransom granted, if they are defeated, then the captive goes free"): **`ransom_granted`** — the body SURVIVED to the clock's end: the automatic resolution PAYS — the court's treasury into the body's supplies (ONE treasury writer, W-COIN; until W-COIN a band step of the court's funding as §9.9's cost) — WHETHER OR NOT the court chose to pay (requirement 24 (b) forces the hand that week; only the DM's DECISION overrides it), or the court PAID EARLY (`pay` at any week ends the race that week, dearer by trust); the captive RETURNS; a DEED on the body's ledger, its notoriety up; **`captive_freed`** — the body was DEFEATED first (a hunt's `snuffed_out` or `driven_off`, a rival body, starvation), or the captive broken out (below) — unpaid, the FREER's deed (the warden's, the paragon's, the party's if the DM marks it); **`captive_released`** — NEVER an automatic close: only the body's OWN early release when its supplies can no longer hold the captive (the captor's landed `release`, receipted, notoriety only), or the DM's decision. The court's engine line on the row is the landed `pay · refuse · abandon`: `pay` closes the race early; `refuse` only bargains and delays (the clock runs, and survival still pays at its end); `abandon` writes the captive off at home — the court will not pay, yet the clock's end still grants the ransom unless the DM decides otherwise, and a captive who returns abandoned carries the LANDED grievance `ABANDONMENT_GRIEVANCE_KIND = 'abandoned_to_captivity'` (its first producer for a body's captive).
- `captive_freed` means "FREED BY AN OUTSIDE HAND before the clock" — the body DEFEATED first (a hunt's `snuffed_out` or `driven_off`, a rival body, starvation — as above) OR THE CAPTIVE BROKEN OUT (THE OPPORTUNITY's `rescue_attempt` verdict `freed` on a body's captive, OP-7, over ES-2b's generalized custody row) — unpaid, the FREER's deed (for a breakout OP's `opportunity_act` / `captive_rescued`, targeted at the jailer — here the body); the closing words stay THREE (`ransom_granted · captive_freed · captive_released`), no fourth word; DARK with OP-7 until ES-2b's custody wave and `roadRescueEnabled`.
- **THE CLOCKED CARD (25b):** the race keeps ONE card on the docket EVERY week of its clock — STANDING, its weekly line "the Hollow Road gang still holds Kell's envoy; the court has refused", its current default — open to the DM at any week (leave — wait · tilt: pay early, order the hunt · decide); it is STANDING until its climax week, CLIMAX at the clock's end, and an EARLY act (the court's `pay`, the hunt's victory, the DM's decision) CLOSES it that week. Nothing queues; the push budget counts the card once.
- THE PRECEDENCE — ONE CAPTIVE, TWO CARDS IN ONE WEEK (a breakout is one act; the rescuer's `rescue_attempt` is CLIMAX — THE OPPORTUNITY's kind, OP §10.1 — and this race's clocked card is STANDING the same week, as is WR-7d's standing `ransom` row for a foreign-guest hold): THE RESCUE RESOLVES FIRST in the week's order — OP's stage runs before this race's resolver and before WR-7d's standing ransom row (OP §2.1's call site); a DM SEAL ON THE RACE that week (`pay` · `hunt` · `wait`) WITHDRAWS the attempt as `refused` — the captive's own terms stand, and the rescuer finds the matter settled; a DM SEAL ON THE ATTEMPT BINDS THE RACE — `caught` → the race stands, now with two captives (the caught rescuer held as OP §5.3 writes him — ≤ 2 named captives per body, `held` §2.2, still binds; a PARAGON's `caught` writes no hold until the owner's errand-row slot and ES-2b pair, OP §9 THE ACT, and then the race stands with its one captive); `freed` → the race closes `captive_freed` (the widened word, above); with neither sealed, the ENGINE DEFAULT runs the attempt first and the race reads its closing. Silence never decides between the two cards: the order does. OP §10.1 carries the same words (OD-OP-37); the fold's `ransom` and `rescue_attempt` registry rows note the precedence. DARK with OP-7 until ES-2b's custody wave and `roadRescueEnabled`. Vetoable.
- **THE ENGINE NEVER HARMS THE CAPTIVE** (no named fate): the captive's fate past release is the DM's.
- **The landed roads hold is not this:** a ROSTER envoy taken `hostage` by the frozen gauntlet's T3 arm (§9.5) stays the LANDED `RansomRec` with its own captor (the hop's town) and its own landed ransom economy; BD only names the hand.

### 9.11 THE HUNT, LED — a mission to snuff out a body (L-BD 40 · 41; the owner, 18:32 and 18:33)
- **The quarry:** a NAMED body (BD23) whose court stance is `hunted` (a gang) or `outlawed` (a flock), or a beast pack — a pack's threat read by its BELIEVED HARM in the court's land, not by fame (a beast has no RN key, §2.5 `bodyThreat01`), so the card mints for the owner's "gang, beast, or conclave" alike. ⭐ A `curbed` flock is WATCHED, never hunted (PR §5.5's quarry rule, which binds a mission too): L-BD 40's "`suppressed`/`outlawed`" reads `outlawed` alone (PR's quarry rule).
- **TAKING THE MISSION is BD's card `hunt_taken`** (§12; STANDING, CLOCKED by the mission's life; WHERE the court's settlement; never PR's — PR mints no kind; CH's champion arm rides the same card — CH's leaf `paragonHunt.js :: championForHunt(worldState, courtId, bodyId, tick) → wnpcId | null` supplies preference (2); the card and its detachment light with `bodyEffectsEnabled` — they draw PR's riders and the treasury's upkeep): DEFAULT — the court's PUBLIC-SECURITY office-holder takes it (`undertaken`) the week the body's `bodyThreat01` at that court crosses `HUNT_THREAT_FLOOR` (DRAFT 0.6, UNMEASURED); the DM may NAME another leader (a paragon), STOP it (`stood_down`), or decide its close. The card names WHO leads and WITH WHAT.
- **THE LEADER, by preference:** (1) THE OFFICE — whoever holds public security in that government (the owner: "prefernce given to wardens and nobles or whowever is in charge of public security"), in this order: (i) a person of THE WARDEN ARCHETYPE HOLDING the office (a paragon — the one CH's `securityOfficeOf(worldState, courtId)` returns, then `isWardenParagon` on it — or a non-paragon office-holder whose roster role is `warden` — §2.3); (ii) the SWORD-HOLDER by L-BD 23's ONE read (`SWORD_BY_GOVERNMENT`, §10.3 — the faction), its PERSON the faction's leader seat (`roleCatalog.js :: FACTION_SEAT_ROLES`' "`{ role: 'Leader', importance: 'pillar', seat: 'leader_champion' }`"): a noble in a feudal town, the militia's master in a guild republic, the temple's guard in a theocracy, the council's captain in a free town; (iii) where that seat is empty, the town's WATCH — the `law_enforcement` senior seat (`INSTITUTION_ROLES`: "`law_enforcement: [{ role: 'Watch Captain', importance: 'pillar' }`") (`cure/D-BD-r4.md` m5); (2) a RETAINED paragon of the court (CH's retainer); (3) a FREE paragon or a CORPS answering the BOUNTY (§9.9 — a corps hunts with its OWN unit).
- **THE UNIT, "appropriate to scale":** the body's believed band PLUS ONE — for a gang, a flock or a pack, THE PATROLS' DETACHMENT under the named leader (PR §5.5: `pursuedRef` the body, `leaderRef` the leader; the deploy-time draw; never more than `fielded − 1`); for a body at MENACE scale (it is then MN's — BD16) a COLUMN under a GENERAL (the generals design, C4's substrate; CITED, never duplicated). The scale rule is ONE table by size band (DRAFT).
- **THE PURSUIT** is THE HUNT of §9.3 (PR §5.5's text), LED: the leader's axes and lessons shape the prediction and the engagement choice (a cautious warden groups and waits; a glory-seeking noble pursues too far — over-pursuit its price; HB's person grain where it lights). A paragon leader moves by its OWN mover (CH's purpose `hunt_body` with the body as its target — CH §4.2; `hunt` is LANDED and is this volume's court↔beast stance word; two movers never share a record), reading the ONE prediction `predictedPlaceOf`. A pursuing unit GLEANS the way the body fled by — node by node, PR's gleaning roll, BD's write (`by_pursuit`, §2.3; L-BD 11e) — unless its court bought the way from a broker first (`by_broker`, L-BD 11g). The ENGAGEMENT is the EXISTING hearing in its week (`patrol_engages` on the road; ST's site kinds at a den, lair or shrine; never `bodies_meet` — the unit is not a body).
- **THE WARDEN'S WAYS (L-BD 41):** a hunt led by THE WARDEN (the public-security office held by a person of the warden archetype — CH's `isWardenParagon` or the roster role `warden`, §2.3) rides the HIDDEN WAYS of its jurisdiction BY ITS HOLDER'S KNOWLEDGE READ (`knownTrailsOf`'s office arm; never a traveller token — PR's `patrol` token enters neither array, §5.2) — its detachment no larger than the way's capacity (`modest`, §5.2: "a body of thirty, never a column"), else it keeps to the road and the junctions.
- **THE STAKE and its closings (the body's words and the unit's — never the person's):** the body `snuffed_out` (ended `scattered` or `starved`), `driven_off` (beaten, its base lost) or `escaped` (the trail went cold); the unit `beaten` or `returned`. Success is ONE DEED, MINTED BY THE HUNT'S OWN DOOR ("the hearing's OWNER mints"): CH's `hunt_won` row at the mission's close (`hunt_taken`'s climax), DOER the LEADER the card named (a paragon or a company through CH's `paragon_act` door; an office-holder on the person ledger by the same registered row — the leader IS the doer, never a `ledBy` rider, RN §2.1), TARGET the body; a `beaten` close (the riders cut down) is the BODY's `fought` deed (§7.2); PR's `patrol_clash` mints NO deed for a LED hunt (its row is the clash's record only), and an UNLED patrol clash mints `patrol_clash`'s own deed with the TOWN as doer (a tale, RN1); a claimed bounty is that same `hunt_won` row (no third deed, §9.9); a hunt that fails as `escaped` is a deed of the BODY's (its notoriety up — RN registers `escaped` beside `hunt_won`); PT's `body_ended` reads `hunt_won`; the office-holder's standing and the court's legitimacy rise by the landed legitimacy writer — §7.3's delta row, one truth; failure is CH's existing `paragon_disgraced` for a paragon and a legitimacy fall for an office-holder. ⭐ THE LEADER NEVER DIES BY THE ENGINE (no named fate): a `cut_down` writes the riders' losses and leaves the leader untouched.
- **COUNTERFORCES:** the riders are off the walls and the ROUND goes UNRIDDEN while the hunt is out (PR §5.5's first counterforce — the gang's chance); the riders' PAY carries the detachment (a detachment is NO deployment record and pays NO deployment upkeep — PR §0.1; the riders' coin is the W-COIN `patrols` row when it lights; a menace-scale COLUMN alone pays deployment upkeep); OVER-PURSUIT; and a FAILED hunt raises the body's NOTORIETY (RN's fame of its key: an `escaped` close is a deed of the body's), which feeds its recruitment (§2.5) — a bad hunt is worse than none.
- **Felt:** "Kell's warden has taken a troop of riders after the Hollow Road gang." (surface, at `undertaken`) · "Kell's warden knows the drover's way; the hill gang has moved its den across the border." (surface) · "The Hollow Road gang slipped Kell's hunt in the fens; it is spoken of in every inn now." (surface, an `escaped` close).

## §10 SPILLOVER, RESPONSIBILITY AND THE NOBLES (BD-6; the owner's ~19:1x "if there is a lot of such embattled region units and activity happening on a map for extended periods of time, they have a chance to amplify them in neighbors … settlemetns need to keep their own peace or be held responsible … nobles as a power share in the good and bad of keeping public order in feudal governments")

### 10.1 Spillover — two landed carriers, never a copy of the level
- **(i) FAST — bodies move, ONE CARRIER PER KIND (the owner, 18:28: "also you have it that beasts and conclave also spill over correct?"):** GANGS by the hidden ways (a pressed gang takes a trail into the next region; trails cross borders); BEASTS by the PACK RANGING across a border over its cover countries (§5.2; its habitat the wilderness extent × the season, so a border with rough country on both sides LEAKS packs); CONCLAVES by MISSIONS (the `sent_forth` birth toward a fractured or receptive neighbour, §3.2 (a); WF's `missionize` objective) and by an OUTLAWED flock MOVING to the friendlier neighbour by believed stance (§5.3). Nothing else is needed for the fast spill.
- **(ii) SLOW — habitat spill**: the LANDED flows already carry a neighbour's trouble into T by their own arithmetic, and BD writes none of them — trade losses on the shared roads (EC/TR's losses read the level), refugees (POP's migration from N into T: the let-in raise T's crime pressure, the cast-out detach gangs, §3.2 (e)), the watch stretched (PR's riders called to the border); on top of them the owner's "they have a chance to amplify them in neighbors" is ONE DRAWN TERM: where a neighbour N has stood `embattled` for DRAFT 13 weeks (the owner's "extended periods of time"), each week ONE keyed chance per (N, T) on the stream `bodies:spill:<N>:<T>:<tick>` (HBF `bodySpillDraw`), its odds by the shared border's length (the digest's gates between N and T) and the trails between them, FIRES at most once per dwell — its tick written to the ledger's `spills` map under `'<N>|<T>'` (§2.2; one writer) — and from that week T's `bodyHabitat` carries a `spill` PART (§2.5) for GANGS and BEASTS: `0.15 × N.level × (1 − age ÷ 13 weeks) × the frontier factor below` (DRAFT; N's level read LIVE each week, so the part falls with N's own easing; `age` the weeks since the fire), ZERO once N leaves `embattled`, the key dropped one dwell after its tick — so a spill raises births and recruitment in T for a season and decays legibly, the receipt naming the source region ("trouble from the Tolle hills has crept into Ashford's country"). The slow habitat spill applies to GANGS and BEASTS (the wilderness's own: a neighbour's prolonged pack season raises T's `wild_born` habitat the same bounded way); for CONCLAVES the slow carrier is THE FAITH ITSELF — WF's spread through the vassal chain and the faith carriers (`religiousContest.js :: faithCarriersOut`; PL17's slowness) — never a second spread law.
- **Counterforce — the frontier:** a held keep on the approach (ST) and the KNOWN trail junctions at the border (§2.3) each halve the spill (DRAFT, floored at a quarter): a town that watches its frontier holds the spill at the line. The map's danger legend keeps reading the one level.

### 10.2 Responsibility — three faces through landed channels (ROUTES PL · GR · PR)
- **(a) Between PEERS:** the EXPORTING town E (the body's base or birth in E's land, or its habitat spill fired) takes ONE typed resentment patch from each neighbour the spill reaches — `relationshipEvolution.js :: applyRelationshipPatch(worldState, { relationshipKey, relationshipPatch: { trust: t − m, resentment: r + m }, metadata: { incidentType: 'peace_unkept', bodyKind, bodyId, fromId }, proposalPayload: null }, now, edge)` — ONE cause family `peace_unkept`, the body's kind its field, never three cause words (L-BD 23b), the landed STATE idiom (`sovereigntyTransfer.js :: accrueSaleGrievance`), `m` DRAFT 0.02, at most once per (pair, body) per DRAFT 13 weeks, on the real graph edge only (no edge, nothing written); the neighbour gains a GRIEVANCE it may press ("keep your peace") through GR's demand grammar — a term producer row in GR's lens and GR's demand hearing in its week, refusal a casus on the landed grievance ladder (cite, never a second ladder; ROUTE GR). THE CONSEQUENCE BY KIND (the owner, 18:28: "beasts consequences being similar ot gangs, conclaves consequences following ideological differences"):
  - **A GANG** — always negative: the patch above, the grievance "keep your peace".
  - **A BEAST** — always negative, with ONE difference: a court cannot HARBOUR a pack (no stance, BD10), so a neighbour holds the exporting territory's holder responsible for NEGLECT — unhunted lairs on its land (ST's site facts: a lair held by a beast attached to E, never cleared), the hunt never ordered (PR's facts: no `pursue` against it in a season) — never for tolerance; the same `peace_unkept` patch (`bodyKind: 'beast'`), the grievance "hunt your wolves", and (b)'s liege default and §10.3's sword-holder move as for a gang.
  - **A CONCLAVE** — SIGNED by the ALIGNMENT DISTANCE (§7.1's one read, `stanceOf`) between the flock's god and the RECEIVING neighbour's PATRON: NEAR (net ≥ 0) → the neighbour WELCOMES the spill: NO grievance and NO resentment patch — and the TRUST a shared faith brings rides ONE NEW BOUNDED LEAN on the landed relationship patch channel: `applyRelationshipPatch(worldState, { relationshipKey, relationshipPatch: { trust: t + m × sharedFaith01 }, metadata: { incidentType: 'shared_faith', bodyId, fromId }, proposalPayload: null }, now, edge)` — `sharedFaith01` the SAME distance `generosityKernel.js :: faithProximity(dG, dR)` computes, in its LANDED CALL SHAPE — the TWO TOWNS' patrons ("from the settlements' primaryDeitySnapshot": `dG` the EXPORTER's `config.primaryDeitySnapshot`, `dR` the receiver's; "`const sharedFaith01 = samePatron ? 1 : clamp01(1 - faithDist);`"), never the flock's god — and ZERO where the exporter holds the flock `curbed` or `outlawed` (a persecutor earns no trust for the flock it drove out); the NEAR/FAR sign itself stays the flock's god against the receiver's patron, §7.1's one read, `m` DRAFT 0.02 (the `peace_unkept` patch's size), at most once per (pair, body) per DRAFT 13 weeks, on the real graph edge only, DECLARED at BD-6 (the cause `shared_faith` FREE on master); `faithProximity` is MODULE-PRIVATE in a host AT the 800 ceiling, so BD-6's car-0 adds an `export` keyword (+0 effective lines) or BD's leaf reads the same two landed axes (`deityAxes.js :: evil01 · chaos01`) by the same formula under a PARITY PIN (the landed "pinned equal" idiom) — one distance, never a second faith law; FAR (net < 0) → the neighbour RESENTS the exporting town for the flock it sends — the `peace_unkept` patch (`bodyKind: 'conclave'`) with `m` scaled by the distance (DRAFT `m × (−net)`, capped at `2m`), and a grievance to press, "keep your priests", by the same GR road. The engine judges no god: the resentment is the neighbour's, read from its own patron's axes.
- **(b) Under a LIEGE:** keeping the peace is part of the member's SERVICE duty: a body based or born in a member's land that spills into a fellow member's or the liege's land is a DEFAULT on the member's obligations — a REQUESTED fealty row `peace_unkept` — the REQUESTED family's (PL §4.8 (1), §8: written under the requester's own flag ∧ `polityEnabled`, i.e. BD's `bodySpillEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled`, its liege face also needing `polityObligationsEnabled`, §13; an obligation by MEANING) — through PL's `requestFealtyRow` (prefix `body:`, the episode `bodyId + ':' + <tick>`, BD composing it); the row carries NO patch and never enters the hold's `P` (PL §4.8 (1), §4.9) — the pair's resentment moves by face (a) ONLY; the crown may ORDER a sweep through PL's precedence — its standing order read by PL's `huntOrderOf` (an input line on each member's `body_stance` fork, §7.1) — and PR's `PATROL_ORDER` by the crown as PR names it (ROUTE PR).
- **(b′) The crown's FAITH decides a flock's sign under a liege (L-BD 23b):** BD REQUESTS ONE of PL §4.8 (4)'s three rows through PL's door on the (liege ↔ member) edge (episode `body:<kind>:<hash>:<tick>`, BD composing it), EACH JUDGED BY THAT EDGE'S LIEGE'S PATRON CREED — every lord heads its own sub-tree, so "the capital's patron" holds only for a direct vassal of the crown: (a) a flock of the LIEGE'S OWN creed spreading from the member is SERVICE — ONE requested `service_answered` credit, no patch — ONLY where the member's court stands `permitted` or `endowed` toward that flock (one that curbs or outlaws it earns nothing); (b) a flock of a RIVAL creed so spreading is DEFIANCE on PL17's fault line ONLY where the member's court has ENDOWED it — ONE requested `fealty_defied` with its deference patch, never a break — and a rival flock merely `permitted`, `curbed` or `outlawed` is the ordinary `peace_unkept` default of (b) when it spreads; (c) THE MIRROR COUNTERFORCE — a member whose court stands `curbed` or `outlawed` toward a flock of its liege's own creed pays ONE requested `fealty_defied` with its deference patch (episode the week that stance took hold), "the crown's protection withdrawn one step" being that price's resentment falling through grip into the deference band the liege's relief fork reads — NEVER a protection row (PL §4.9's one path); a HOSTILE flock's spill (a `curbed`/`outlawed` flock raiding a fellow member) is `peace_unkept` whatever its creed. All three are the REQUESTED family's, under `bodySpillEnabled` ∧ `polityEnabled` with the liege face `polityObligationsEnabled` (§13). Faith is both a bond and a quarrel between towns, by one distance read.
- **(c) The INVERSE CREDIT:** a town that keeps its peace — no body based in its land for DRAFT 26 weeks while a neighbour is embattled — earns trust with its neighbours (the same state idiom, positive, `incidentType: 'peace_kept'`) and legitimacy at home (the one legitimacy writer, bounded DRAFT +1).

### 10.3 The nobles — whoever holds the sword shares in order kept and lost (ROUTE INT)
Read ONCE as the town's RULING FACTION ITSELF — the faction whose archetype set the town's government on the faction plane's own record (PLAUSIBLE until BD-6's car-0 names the field) — never an inverted copy of `factionCompetition.js :: GOVERNMENT_BY_ARCHETYPE`, which is NOT exported ("`const GOVERNMENT_BY_ARCHETYPE`") and NOT injective ("`noble: 'noble_patronage'`" and "`outsider: 'noble_patronage'`": an outsider-ruled town would read as noble); `SWORD_BY_GOVERNMENT` survives only as the DISPLAY gloss of that read (who the sword-holder is called by government): `noble_patronage` → the NOBLE faction (the feudal town — the owner's word) · `military_order` → the military · `temple_authority` → the religious (the temple's guard) · `council_rule` → the civic (the council) · `merchant_charter` → the merchant (the guild's militia masters) · `communal_assembly` → labor · `arcane_magocracy` → arcane · `criminal_shadow_rule` → the criminal (where the underworld rules, the order is its own). ORDER KEPT (a body beaten in the town's land; the level eased; the peace kept) → that faction's POWER SHARE rises (the faction plane's one writer, below) and the TOWN's PUBLIC legitimacy with it (the ONE legitimacy writer, `applyLegitimacyDeltasToUpdates` — §7.3's delta row); ORDER LOST (a long embattlement, an unchecked gang, a village raided `succeeds`) → both fall, and the landed `coup_detat` · `insurgency` · `political_fracture` gates read the PUBLIC legitimacy downstream through their own band rows (`stressorGates.js`: "`const legitimacy = governanceLedger(entry.settlement).legitimacyScore;`", which reads `powerStructure.publicLegitimacy` — never a faction's power); the commons feel it through the reads already downstream — BD writes NO grievance. Through ONE bounded lean (DRAFT ±1 power point a season) on the faction plane's one writer (`factionCompetition.js :: applyFactionPatch`, 775 eff — a LEAF-FIRST car: the lean is computed in BD's leaf `bodies/publicOrder.js` and the host takes at most one call); never a second standing. OVER-POLICING is paid in the commons' grievance through PR's `W_HARASS` (PR §7.5; cite, never a second).

### 10.4 SPONSORSHIP — resources from a foreign power, in secret (BD26; the owner, 18:49: "conclaves and gangs can also attain resources and funds for their operations through foreign powers … it is clandestine of course, and matters of trust and personality")
- **THE TIE (a persisted shape — THE OWNER'S, ⭐ OD-BD-31):** `sponsorships[tieId] = { sponsorRef, bodyId, flow, purpose, carrier, sinceTick, lastDelivery?, exposedAt? }` (§2.2; `exposedAt` the ONE latch per tie) — a CLANDESTINE TIE, NEVER a stance (the land-holder's attitude toward the body stays §7.1's; this is a contract across a border) between a SPONSOR (a foreign COURT; for a conclave also its creed's MOTHER TEMPLE abroad — the creed's institution in the town where it is `ascendant`, WF's) and a CLIENT body (a gang or a conclave; BEASTS NEVER, BD10). `flow` is a BAND on the sponsor's funding (`settlementFundingBand`: the sponsor's purse — coin when W-COIN lights, a treasury line on the sponsor then, the deployments-and-upkeep ledger's slot, so it is never free); `purpose ∈ SPONSOR_PURPOSES = bleed_roads · spread_faith · hold_border` (the sponsor's covert objective: weaken a rival's roads; spread our faith into its town — `spread_faith` is the landed court motive key, and a church seat's `missionize`; hold the keep on its border); `carrier ∈ CARRIER_KINDS` (below); TRUST both ways is RN's regard (`c:<sponsor>` → `g:<bodyId>`; the body's of the sponsor — a gang `g:` holder, a flock its creed's derived regard, RN7), fading with distance and time (RN's decay; no second trust).
- **THE OFFER — ONE COVERT DECIDER, ES's; BD-9 OWNS THE CAR**: the SPONSOR's covert CHARACTER decides it, in ES's ONE covert decider — `espionage/espionageDoctrineStage.js` (the doctrine read `espionageDoctrineFor` and the cadence `dispatchCadenceFor` — its header: this stage answers "does this court want to send somebody, and how sure does it need to be"; 115 effective lines, m1). BD-9's OWNED CAR adds ONE objective there, `sponsor_body`: ONE sibling door in that file, `sponsorObjectiveFor({ worldState, item, courtId, tick, candidate })`, which reads ES's OWN doctrine words — `method` `lawful` ⇒ only a FLOCK of the sponsor's own creed, never a gang; `lawless` ⇒ any gang or flock that bleeds a rival; `targeting` filters the target court (`foes_only` a court it believes `hostile`; `rivals_and_foes` also `rival` · `cold_war`; `all_courts` any) — and ES's own cadence (`frequency01`, the court's covert appetite), and draws ONCE on ES's own keyed stream, `hash01(dispatchCadenceKey(courtId, tick) + ':sponsor_body')` (L1: a keyed hash, zero new PRNG streams), against `frequency01 × score01`; `score01` is the objective's SCORE from BD's PURE leaf `bodies/sponsorObjective.js :: sponsorObjectiveScore(worldState, snapshot, courtId, tick) → { score01, bodyId, targetCourtId, reasons } | null`, bounded `[0, 0.5]` (DRAFT, UNMEASURED): the sponsor's PURSE (ST's `settlementFundingBand` at `funded` or better, else null), its HOSTILITY to the target court READ THROUGH BELIEF (`beliefMap.js :: readBeliefRelationship(sponsorId, targetCourtId, worldState, truthType)` — `hostile` · `rival` · `cold_war`) and the CANDIDATE — a gang or a flock the sponsor BELIEVES works that court's land (`believedBodies(worldState, sponsorId)`, its last place there; never a beast, BD10; never a body already holding two ties, §2.2), the score rising with the pair's believed hostility and the candidate's believed might, its top three reasons on the receipt; null wherever `bodySponsorshipEnabled` is dark (the door then answers `{ sponsor: false, reason: 'dark' }` and nothing is written — byte-identical). ⚠ MEASURED (m2): the decider has NO production caller on master — "a FREE-STANDING spy row still does not exist in this tree", ES's dispatcher wave unchartered (ES §4 THE WAVES) — so THE BODIES' stage is that door's CALLER for this ONE objective, once a SEASON per court whose score is non-null (§2.7 step 6), never its decider; ES's own dispatcher, when it lands, reads the same keyed decision (one decision, two readers, consumption-independent) (the existing mission chooser is a DECISION without a caller; the car supplies the caller for its own objective and for nothing else — vetoable; the alternative is the offer DARK until ES's dispatcher is chartered). No errand row carries the offer (a covert row rides only a diplomatic errand's face): THE OFFER IS ONE EVENT, macro over micro like the delivery — the week after a `sponsor` verdict a go-between of the sponsor's (a receipt, never a named person) meets the body at its base or on its ways (§5.2), and its card mints. The BODY answers in its own week: a STANDING card `sponsorship_offered` (§12), CLOCKED while pending (BD13), WHERE the body's site or country; DEFAULT `accepted · refused` by NEED (supplies: a starving gang takes any coin) × PERSONALITY (a good flock refuses an evil god's silver; a lawful one refuses a far-alignment sponsor — §4.2's god read; a gang's leader's greed and loyalty, §6.10) × TRUST (what the body believes of that court, `believedStanceOf` and RN's regard); the DM may decide.
- **WHAT THE FLOW DOES:** it feeds SUPPLIES (§6.1) — the ONE legitimate way past what the body's habitat would feed (§3.4; bounded by the sponsor's purse), and so its readiness; the PURPOSE steers the body's weekly choice ONLY AS A WEIGHT (§4.1 (iii): a sponsored gang leans toward the rival's roads, a sponsored flock toward `supplicate` and `preach` in the rival's town) — the body KEEPS ITS MIND. COMPLIANCE = trust × personality: at low trust a gang POCKETS the coin and does nothing, a flock ENDS the tie. TRUST moves by DELIVERY (the body's regard of the sponsor rises with pay) and by RESULT (the sponsor's of the body); BETRAYAL is a DEED on RN's ledger (a gang SELLS the sponsor's secret to the host court — the informer's road, an exposure below; a sponsor ABANDONS a client mid-war).
- **THE DELIVERY — macro over micro (the owner, 18:52: "how does the money and resources get there? … is an NPC invovled or not?"):** never tracked coin — ONE DELIVERY EVENT per season per tie (§2.7 step 2) with a CARRIER, a ROUTE (the hidden ways; or the overt road where the carrier is innocuous) and a CATCH ROLL — THE PATROLS' one encounter law on truth (coverage × cover × the carrier's competence, ES's `espionageMath.js :: covertCompetence01`), receipted — the competence term only for a NAMED carrier (the operative, dark until ES carries it); a smuggler's load is the gauntlet's own catch and a cache the junction's watch (with the operative dark, `covertCompetence01(homeSettlement, npc)` has no person to read on a live carrier). FOUR CARRIERS (`CARRIER_KINDS`), an NPC in two of them — TWO LIVE (`smuggler` · `cache_drop`) until ES and IN carry the other two (the four words stay the save-data contract):
  - (a) `operative` — ⛔ DARK UNTIL ES CARRIES IT (ES's errand spine has no courier errand and no free-standing covert row, m2; the carrier widens back by an ES unit that carries a courier errand — a fold §3.1 ES INBOX row; never chosen while dark) — a NAMED person on ES's errand spine (ES's existing operative kinds; its own loyalty and personality) carries the coin by hand on the ways; CATCHABLE by the encounter law; a named catch is CUSTODY (ES-2b, the owner's slot); what the host LEARNS is what the operative TELLS (ES's own interrogation and loyalty read — belief, never the tie's truth; ROUTE ES).
  - (b) `smuggler` — UNNAMED, a goods flow through EC's GAUNTLET with the silver as CONTRABAND (THE PATROLS' item-16 smuggling parity); cheaper and deniable; SEIZED rather than confessed — the seizure itself is the leak's evidence ("Tolle's silver").
  - (c) `cache_drop` — coin left at a SITE (a shrine, a ruin) or at a way's JUNCTION, picked up by the body; no person to catch; a patrol that KNOWS the junction (§2.3 — the warden's office among them) FINDS the drop on its round.
  - (d) `temple_alms` — ⛔ DARK UNTIL IN (AND WF-7) CARRY IT (IN carries no scrutiny row for a temple's tithe and WF-7's purse has no writer; the carrier widens back by an IN unit carrying the scrutiny roll — a fold §3.1 IN INBOX row — and WF-7 the purse; a mother temple sponsors by the two live carriers meanwhile) — for CONCLAVES only, ALMS from a foreign mother temple through the faith's own institutions as tithe — it looks legitimate until the AMOUNTS are noticed (IN: a brokerage or an operative reads the ledger — a leak by SCRUTINY, not by capture); the temple purse is WF-7's slot (`templeWealth` has no writer, m1), so until WF-7 the carrier's catch is IN's scrutiny roll alone (ROUTE WF · IN).
  The SPONSOR'S court CHOOSES the carrier by its personality and the stakes AMONG THE LIVE TWO — a careful `lawful` court (ES's method word) leaves a cache at a junction it believes unwatched; a cheap `lawless` one sends a smuggler's load; a mother temple the same two until IN and WF-7 carry its alms — the operative and the alms WIDEN the choice back to four by their ES and IN units — and the choice SETS THE LEAK'S ODDS: the carrier's catch roll plus ES-6a's standing leak. No second courier law: the gauntlet and the site record ARE the live carriers (the errand spine joins them with ES's courier unit).
- **THE LEAK — three things move at once:** the tie carries ES-6a's leak chance and the carrier's catch; its exposure rides CH's ONE clandestine idiom — fired ONLY through CH's SHARED LEAF `exposeCovertHire` (which calls `corruptionWeb.js :: applyForeignExposureBlowback` with the ONE source `covert_hire_exposed`; the tie's kind carried as `exposeCovertHire`'s `tieKind` — `employed` · `sponsorship`, BD's two in CH's set `retainer · employed · sponsorship` — on the receipt and the `covert_hire_exposed` deed, never the blowback source (CH §9); called only where CH's covert arm is lit; no second exposure source), ONE LATCH per tie — `exposedAt` on the tie, written by BD's writer in the act that fires it — and RN's `TELLING_SOURCES` row (`leak:body:<bodyId>:<courtId>:<sinceTick>`, `courtId` the sponsoring court, ONE PER TIE — RN's registry spelling; its content CH's `paragon_leak` with `bodyId`/`bodyKind`; ROUTE RN). EACH CAUGHT COURIER (a delivery whose catch roll fired, §10.4's carriers) seeds that leak telling at the CATCHING town — the record CH's ONE belief pass reads (it reads every subject's leak arrivals; BD never writes `paragonBeliefs`). On a leak: (i) the host town's RESPONSIBILITY for the spill RE-TARGETS to the sponsor (§10.2 (a)'s `peace_unkept` patch and grievance re-aimed at the sponsor's court — the host is not blamed for sponsored harm it can show); (ii) the host gains a CASUS on the LANDED grievance ladder against the sponsor (never a second ladder) through a CLIMAX hearing `sponsor_exposed` for the host court (§12): `demand_made · casus_taken · let_lie`; (iii) the host HUNTS the body harder (its fork pressed to `hunted`, §7.1). A sponsor whose own commons learn it paid brigands abroad pays in LEGITIMACY at home (INT; the credibility stock's `deception` charge the clandestine idiom already levies).
- **COUNTERFORCES:** the purse · the leak · betrayal · the host's hunt · trust decaying with distance · the sponsor's own people.
- **Felt (one sentence for the DM):** "Tolle's silver was found in the Hollow Road gang's den." (surface, at the leak; before it, the scrutiny walk names no sponsor — belief never truth).

### 10.5 COLD WAR — proxies in peace, half-open in war (the owner, 18:50: "cold war tactics both during war and in peace time")
- **IN PEACE** sponsorship is the DENIABLE WEAPON: a court without a casus bleeds its rival through PROXIES (a sponsored gang on its roads; a sponsored flock against its patron's legitimacy; a RUMOR CAMPAIGN preached from a sponsored pulpit through IN's statecraft — `infoStatecraftEnabled`'s instruments, cited, never a second rumor law).
- **THE LEAK IS THE ESCALATION LADDER:** leak → the rival's demand (GR's demand hearing) → a refused demand is a CASUS on the landed ladder → the war the sponsor avoided. Proxies breed COUNTER-PROXIES (the rival sponsors back — bounded by both purses and both leaks: ≤ 2 ties per body, ≤ 4 per sponsor, §2.2).
- **DÉTENTE WITH TEETH:** GR's pact grammar gains ONE CLAUSE against sponsorship ("no hand in each other's hills") on the landed treaty shape — ONE new term on `peaceTermsCatalog.js :: TERM_CATALOG` (GR V-2: "12 terms / 8 families"; measured, m12), whose LEAKED violation is its own casus (ROUTE GR).
- **THE SPHERE:** inside a kingdom the crown's reach bounds foreign sponsorship — sponsoring a body in a rival crown's MEMBER affronts the CROWN through THE BANNER's protection: the crown named is `polityOf(worldState, snapshot, memberId).rootId` (the banner stack uncut), affronted only while its OWN read of its hold is `staunch` or firmer (PL §4.8 (5)); BD's leak writes the crown's casus as well as the member's on the landed grievance ladder — PL writes no row.
- **IN WAR** the same ties come half into the open: sponsored gangs on the enemy's SUPPLY roads (§9.8 (a) · (f)), sponsored flocks against a besieged town's RESOLVE (the war program's home-front costs, `warHomeCosts.js :: applyHomeWarCosts`, cited — ROUTE WC), irregulars (`employed`, §9.8 (a)); the INTELLIGENCE DUEL (ES's operatives against THE PATROLS against the bodies' own informers, §7.6) decides it.
- **ALL BEHIND THE SCENES:** the DM sees the found silver, the preacher from abroad, the treaty broken — never the machinery (requirement 16's sign within weeks: the first sponsored raid; the scrutiny walk names the sponsor only after a leak — belief never truth).

## §11 THE DM'S HAND (each verb ≤ four dials; a realm-manifest row; a proposal whose `targetSaveId` is a REAL settlement — the body's country's anchor — with the body on the `subject` dial; premium; staged then approved, the landed shape of every AUTHOR act)

| Verb | Dials | Hat | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BODY_STANCE` | court · subject (a body, or a creed `creed:<ref>`) · word (the subject kind's `COURT_STANCES` line) | AUTHOR ("it is so") or ORDER (the court's act through the `body_stance` hearing) | sets a court's stance; the body chooses next week |
| `PROMOTE_BODY` | body · the menace's name · its course | AUTHOR | stages ONE `RAISE_MENACE` proposal in the same act, its four dials filled from the body as MN §2 writes it — `name` the DM's words (MN12: the DM names the menace; a NAMED body's name is OFFERED as the dial's DEFAULT, kept or changed by the DM; a nameless body takes the DM's name), `where` the body's place in MN's WHERE words (the country of the settlement its held site is attached to, or the country it stands in, or the road `a\|b` it holds — the landed `pairKey` form (MN's `a␟b` is routed back to MN); a body on a WAY names the NODE COUNTRY the way comes out at — its nearer junction's end — never a way, which is no MN place), `might` its might key (the one `STRENGTH_BAND_KEYS` ladder), `posture` the course where it is `stays` or `besieges` (any other course — `toward`, `wanders`, `enlists` — is STAGED by MN's `RAISE_MENACE` apply arm the tick the menace exists, read from this act's payload; never a `MENACE_COURSE` row in the same act, whose subject `menaceOptions` cannot yet hold — MN §2, m4); then ends the body `raised` (BD16) and its den passes to the menace through ST's writer — THE PROMOTION VERB (OD-BD-6, the owner's) |
| `BODY_HOLD` | body · hold · release | AUTHOR | pins a body: no choice, no birth from it, no end but the DM's |
| `END_BODY` | body · party | AUTHOR | ends it `disbanded` (CH's "DEATH = DISBANDMENT, THE DM'S" idiom); with the `party` dial ON (the party ended it at the table), the verb's EXECUTION calls PT's door — the deed `body_ended`, doer the party, target `{ kind: 'body', id }` (PT §3.1; `causedBy` the verb's outcome id) |
| `PLACE_BODY` | kind (`gang` · `conclave` · `beast`) · anchor · might · name | AUTHOR | the DM places a body — the owner's "AUTHOR a body (kind · place · size · stance)" (L-BD 36; the STANCE is the court's, set by `BODY_STANCE`, so the four dials stay four); a `name` the DM's words (a gang or a flock only); a BEAST is placed NAMELESS and is the world's own from the week after (no course, no target) — a pack the DM NAMES or DRIVES is a MENACE (MN's `RAISE_MENACE`; BD10) (OD-BD-25) |
| `TRAIL_KNOWN` | town · trail · known / forgotten · party | AUTHOR | the DM tells a TOWN of a way (`by_dm`, §2.3) or, with the `party` dial ON, REVEALS it TO THE PARTY — the execution calls PT's door, `party_made_known_trail` (a mark on THE PARTY's record — PT's shape and writer, read by nothing in the world) — the party's own discoveries are the DM's to tell |
| `BODY_LEADER` | body · paragon (a `wnpc_` with CH's mark) · install / remove | AUTHOR | NAMES a named gang's or flock's LEADER by writing THROUGH CH's leaf `paragonRetainer.js :: setLeader` (CH's field `paragon.leads`, THE record; custom content; §6.10); removal is the leader's loss (§6.4 — cohesion falls, fission is tested) |
| `BODY_GOAL` | body · end goal (`GANG_END_GOALS`) | AUTHOR | sets a gang's one end (§6.8) |

The owner's six verbs over bodies (L-BD 36: AUTHOR · SET a court's stance · NAME its leader · DISBAND · PROMOTE · REVEAL a trail to the party) are `PLACE_BODY` · `BODY_STANCE` · `BODY_LEADER` · `END_BODY` · `PROMOTE_BODY` · `TRAIL_KNOWN`; `BODY_HOLD` and `BODY_GOAL` are the two the volume adds (eight rows, ROUTE CN for the console's verb list). AN AUTHORED EVENT involving a body is a verb plus a decision on the resulting card — never a mechanism: "the gang attacks the mill" is `PLACE_BODY` (or an existing body) + a `raid` DECIDED on its card (L-BD 49 (a)). Undo is the proposal ring's snapshot restore (the landed undo; inverses are DM acts). Edit Mode reaches every field of the record (the DM's total control — the owner's safety net: "surprise is safe because the DM can undo any advance, edit any state and decide any hearing").

- **The three choices** (requirement 24): at a ONE-WEEK advance the DM may LEAVE the week to the engine, TILT it, or DECIDE it (the DM's word is the verdict; never "decree"); the hearing seal is read FIRST by each resolver. **Only on the week:** every kind is minted only at a one-week advance and lives that week; the next advance resolves it first; a MONTH, SEASON or YEAR advance resolves every week on the default and leaves each week's receipt ("resolved by the engine's default"); nothing queues. **The docket:** BD registers its three resolvers with SP-9 (`registerEngagementKind('bodies_meet', …)` etc.) and its PRODUCER rows into the five widened kinds in the owning volumes' registration lists (BD hosts no docket pass). With `hearingWeekEnabled` dark the defaults resolve in their tick.
- **THE CLOCKED CARD (the owner, 18:55: "the hearing is still avaialble to the DM until its automatically resolved"):** a kind marked CLOCKED keeps ONE card on the docket EVERY week of its clock — STANDING, a weekly line and its current default — available to the DM at ANY week (leave: the clock runs on defaults · tilt · decide); it CLOSES at its AUTOMATIC resolution, which is its ONE CLIMAX week (requirement 24(b) binds that week), or EARLY the week the DM's act (pay · hunt · decide) closes it; nothing queues; nothing is decided for the DM; the push budget counts the card ONCE (its weekly line is a scrutiny line, never pushed). The fold's §3.4 rows for these kinds carry `clock` beside `CLIMAX/STANDING` (ROUTE FOLD).
- **No party kind is BD's;** requirement 24's party default never arises in BD's own kinds. **Not hearings:** births, ends, upkeep, recruitment, the weekly choice, a flow's fired loss (its receipt), cohesion and supplies, the spill's chance, the trails learned — receipts only (they decide nothing between two parties). **The push budget** binds: STANDING cards are never pushed; CLIMAX cards are ranked by focus (the party's position, the DM's pins).
- **The card** (the Evidence Law): "The Tolle gang has come down on Low Ford for its grain. Low Ford's men are few; left alone, the gang takes what it came for." · "The Grey Lady's faithful ask Merewick's court for leave to keep a shrine. Left alone, the court refuses." On scrutiny the card names its READ (the body's size and supply word against the village's defence words; the court's fork's reasons — the gods' alignments, the followers in town).
- **The registry count** rises by NINE kind names in nine rows — the first three (`bodies_meet` · `body_stance` · `traffic_waylaid`) and six more (`alms_requested` · `body_bounty` · `hunt_taken` · `guild_war` · `sponsorship_offered` · `sponsor_exposed`) — and the fold's §3.4 recount says the same ("ADDS NINE kind names in nine live rows"); SEVEN existing kinds gain a body as a party (`raid` · `patrol_engages` · `site_taken` · `site_cleared` · `site_siege` · `caravan_seized` · `supply_raid`) and two more (`ransom`, the captor; ST's new `site_sale`, the answerer). ⚠ THE PUSH BUDGET'S ANSWER (counted first): of the nine new kinds FIVE are STANDING (never pushed: `traffic_waylaid` · `alms_requested` · `hunt_taken` · `guild_war` · `sponsorship_offered`), every nameless body makes none (BD23), alms ask once a season, and the bounty and the exposure are rare CLIMAX acts — C0's wartime docket count (three seeds) measures the rest before any of these light.
